Has a footballer ever run a marathon faster than James Milner? | The Knowledge

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Plus: managers sacked after signing new deals, war victims commemorated on kits and the first club website (part II)

Mail us with all of your questions and answers

It came as no surprise to the Knowledge that James Milner had spent the early months of his retirement training for – and then running in – the New York City Marathon. Yes, the bleep test-loving former Brighton, Liverpool and Manchester City player recorded an excellent time of 3hr 9min 4sec, an average of 7min 13sec per mile. Not bad for a 40-year-old whose physique is more like that of a wrestler than a whippet. “A few years ago, I feel like I could have definitely done sub-3:00,” he said, lamenting his late-career injuries for hindering him.

In 2012 we ran a column listing footballers’ fastest marathon times, with the former Leicester playmaker Muzzy Izzet leading the pack on 3hr 22min 36sec at the 2011 London Marathon. That very respectable time has now been surpassed by the Premier League’s record appearance maker, so we thought we had better take a gulp of our isotonic energy drink and run through a few more tales of football’s marathon men and women.

More recent marathon attempts by either active or former footballers puts Izzet down in 12th place, just behind André Schürrle, who clocked 3hr 21min 25sec in Berlin last year, though clearly wanted a greater challenge than a mere 26.2 miles. This year, the 35-year-old completed the 167.7-mile six-stage Marathon des Sables in 51hr 41min 16sec, placing 675th overall. He also climbed Mont Blanc. But back to marathons, where the fastest time we can find for a female footballer is 3hr 40min 30sec by the former USA midfielder Lauren Holiday. She is closely followed by her good friends from the USWNT, Leslie Osborne (3hr 41min 33sec) and Abby Wambach (3hr 44min 25sec).

The Fulham manager, Álvaro Arbeloa, is 11th on our overall list (3hr 15min 21sec, Valencia 2019), with Kevin Kilbane, who always looked like he had a marathon runner’s spring in his step, 10th on 3hr 14min 44sec (London, 2013). A tip of the hat now to Danny Mills, father to the GB Olympic runner George Mills, with Mills Sr running the 2023 London Marathon in 3hr 12min 18sec at 45 years of age. Meanwhile, Milner’s run places him eighth as we approach some of football’s more serious runners.

The former Dundee United and West Ham defender Christian Dailly clocked a seriously impressive 3hr 6min 26sec at the 2013 London Marathon. “In 1990, I went from boys’ football in May to my Dundee United debut in August,” said Dailly after the race. “We’d be down at the beach, and all of a sudden, I’d be running through the sea at St Andrews or up and down the sand dunes. When I got to pre-season at Dundee United, I was flying, running away from everyone.” Dailly went into athletics coaching when his football career ended. We did not expect a goalkeepeer to be jostling for position towards the front of the pack, but the former Derby County and Sheffield Wednesday stopper Lee Grant hared around London in 3hr 0min 33sec this year.

And so we get to the hallowed ground of a sub-three hour marathon and a select group of only five. The Real Madrid legend Raúl González was clearly being cheered from the rafters as he ran his home marathon in 2hr 59min 22sec in 2019. “It was only the second marathon of my life. I wanted to break three hours, and I’m very happy with what I’ve accomplished,” he said. The Oxford United manager, Aaron Ramsey, used his relative youth at 35 to tear up the Stockholm Marathon this summer in exactly 2hr 59min. Arjen Robben was known for his blistering pace over short distances in his glittering career but he showed that he could blend speed with stamina as he raced round Rotterdam in 2023 in a time of 2hr 58min 33sec.

Before setting out on his managerial career, Luis Enrique trained like a demon for the Florence Marathon, with his hard work paying off as he came home in 2hr 58min 8sec. He has since continued his fitness regime, competing in the Frankfurt Ironman event and the Marathon des Sables.

But the medal for the fastest former footballer to run a marathon goes to Alexei Smertin. The former Chelsea and Russia utility man took endurance running seriously when he retired in 2008 and hit a peak of 2hr 48min 17sec at the Kazan Marathon in 2020, aged 45. That is an incredible pace of 6min 25sec per mile. He has since tried switching to longer distances as he has aged but believes he may still have one or two quick marathons left in him. “I’ve been leaning toward ultras. One reason is that speed declines with age,” he said recently. “I thought I’d never be able to improve my personal best [2:48:17] … [but] I have a few more years to improve my speed.”

“What is the soonest a manager has been sacked after signing a contract extension?” asks Dave Smythe.

Adam Clark comes in hot with the first suggestion: “Leam Richardson, then of Wigan Athletic, signed a three-year contract extension on 25 October 2022 and was sacked 16 days later on 10 November 2022. Wigan then recruited Kolo Touré on a three-and-a-half year contract, who subsequently lasted just nine games.”

“I’m pretty sure when Watford sacked Glenn Roeder in 1996 he’d signed a contract extension about a fortnight earlier?” suggests Daz Pearce. Good shout, Daz: Watford were not yet in their era of sacking a manager every month, but Roeder – initially appointed at Vicarage Road in 1993 – was given the boot by Watford just 10 days after signing a new one-year contract extension in February 1996. “I am sure I have learned from this experience,” said Roeder, who announced his intention to stay in the game. He would not get another managerial job for another five years, enjoying a successful stint at West Ham from 2001-03.

Not a contract extension but Kevin Cullis’s appointment at Swansea City in 1996 lasted just seven days. Cullis, who never played professionally, had only ever previously coached non-league Crawley Town’s youth team and was later jailed for fraud in 2003, and didn’t get off to the best start in Wales with Swans players reportedly ignoring his tactics at half-time against Blackpool during just his second game in charge. Cullis resigned shortly afterwards.

Elsewhere, Dave Bassett lasted less than four days at Crystal Palace in 1984, Marcelo Bielsa resigned as manager of Lazio after just two days in the job in 2016 and Leroy Rosenior’s 10-minute stint at Torquay United is Knowledge folklore. You can read more about that here.

We have received some new responses to last week’s Knowledge archive, which features some 2016 answers to the question: who were the first football club to have an official website?

“Last week, Martyn Amos reported an Ipswich fan website started in 1990,” wrote a sceptical Alan Terlep (and others), “but history records that the first known website went online in August 1991, so if that’s correct he has a claim to fame well beyond the Knowledge. A fan site that was already active in 1993 may well be the oldest or there though.”

Fellow reader Mark Francome, who works at a global IT service provider so should know a thing or two about this, emails that “Luton Town fans had ‘WHOSH’ (‘Worldwide Hatters On The Super Highway’), which dates from 1994,” while “Millwall also had ‘You’ll Never Make The Station’ from around the same time. No records exist to confirm the internet rumour that Millwall admins hacked their Luton counterparts and ripped up the HTML.”

“Portsmouth’s new kit has names of fallen soldiers on it. Are there other examples of clubs recognising war victims on their kit?” asked Simon Cook in August 2014.

Andrew Walker points us towards Raith Rovers’ away kit for the 2014-15 season, which has been designed to pay tribute to the 16th Royal Scots regiment, a battalion formed of sportsmen, seven of whom came from the Kirkcaldy club. Three Rovers players – Jimmy Todd, Jimmy Scott and George McLay – were killed during the conflict.

“We wanted to do something completely different for next season in terms of kit colours,” the Raith chair, Turnbull Hutton, told the Dundee Courier,” but at the same time the board has been considering a suitable way to mark this centenary in an appropriate manner, so we have brought these two ambitions together with our new green and black strip. “We hope that it captures the imagination of the Scottish footballing public and, through next season’s sports pages, that it ensures that we really do remember the sacrifices of our players and so many others 100 years ago.”

And in Wales, Aberystwyth Town are also commemorating the 100th anniversary of the outbreak of the first world war. “We’re the kit suppliers to Aberystwyth Town Football Club in the Welsh Premier League,” write the good folk at RM Teamwear, “and this season we produced their home strip honouring the centenary of the first world war, more specifically on the sleeves we have the names of players who played for the club and lost their lives in the Great War. Their name, rank and regiment are on players 1-14 in the war veteran player’s position he used to play. Others have “In Memory of Players of This Club Who Fell in the Great War” as seen in the attached photo.”

“Not only were Rangers v Dundee United and Manchester City v Barnsley in 2004-05 the only British league matches to finish 7-1 that season, the same opposing goalkeepers also played in both matches,” flags Stephen Toal. “Ronald Waterreus played for Rangers and Manchester City while Nick Colgan turned out for both Dundee United and Barnsley. Are there any other examples of opposing goalkeepers coming up against each other twice in the same season for a different set of opposing teams?”

“Following Coventry City’s 3-1 victory over Sunderland, I noticed a potentially unique statistic involving Luke O’Nien, who has now played in defeats against Coventry in all four divisions of English league football. Is this a first?” ponders Kirk Savage.

“Has ever been a headed goal awarded goal of the season?” wonders Rupert Sheard. “I can think of a few belters, but awards tend to go to ones kicked in.”

“Which club team has had the most international captains playing together at one time?” asks MH Zahid. “Conversely, which international team has had the most club captains in its starting lineup at one time?”

I finally saw the Slate Truck — 7 things that really stood out to me about this $25,000 EV

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Over the past year, I’ve tested a handful of “affordable” EVs — which are, in a relative sense, priced lower than the national average of around $50,000. Depending on who you talk to, options below $40,000 look genuinely attractive, and anything under that threshold almost feels too good to be true.

Yet, that’s exactly where the Slate Truck lands with its $24,950 starting price. For an all-electric pickup, I have to admit I’m impressed by the genuine real-world capability it delivers on paper: a rear-mounted motor pushing 181 horsepower and 195 lb-ft of torque, a 65 kWh battery pack, 1,500 pounds of payload capacity, NACS DC fast-charging compatibility, and an estimated 205 miles of range.

While those specs might look modest next to six-figure behemoths like the Rivian R1T, Chevrolet Silverado EV, and GMC Sierra EV, you can't ignore just how accessible that price point is. But after getting up close with the Slate Truck in person, a few details caught me completely off guard. Here’s what stood out the most.

Prior to seeing the Slate Truck in person, I already knew it was scaling things back with crank windows and manual seats. Even in 2026, I still come across a handful of cars with manual seats — though that’s usually reserved strictly for the passenger side, and it's rarer still to find both seats manual.

So while manual adjustment wasn’t a shock, I was taken aback to find that the side mirrors are entirely manual, too. I can live with crank windows and mechanical seat levers, but manual side mirrors? That’s a tougher pill to swallow, simply because it’s been ages since I’ve had to adjust a mirror by hand.

The Slate Truck I saw featured a limited-edition wrap created in collaboration with Smarties. Yes, the candy rolls you probably remember from childhood.

Even though the "Blank Slate" base model rolls off the assembly line with a generic gray exterior, Slate sets itself apart by offering bold, fun vinyl wraps right out of the gate. It’s an ingenious way to make the truck feel uniquely yours, especially with options to match your personal style.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

I’m just curious to see how well these wraps hold up after enduring a few harsh Northeast winters.

Compact pickups are already at a disadvantage due to their smaller footprint, and in a two-seater like the Slate Truck, cabin space is especially tight. Since the seats sit flush against the barrier separating the cabin from the flatbed, there’s practically no leeway to recline them for a more relaxed driving position.

That might not be an issue if the Slate Truck is strictly kept to quick local errands, but plenty of drivers will want to take it on longer road trips. Fighting driver fatigue starts with comfortable ergonomics, so the lack of recline here makes long stretches behind the wheel tough to manage.

I’ve seen deeper, longer beds on full-size pickups, but the Slate Truck’s usable cargo space rivals compact pickups I’ve tested, like the Hyundai Santa Cruz.

Personally, I think the bed is the perfect size for everyday needs — more than spacious enough for weekly Costco runs, while still handling the occasional large piece of furniture without breaking a sweat.

For many drivers, maneuvering into a tight parking spot is an everyday source of stress. Given the Slate Truck’s aggressive cost-cutting measures, I worried that a backup camera might have been left out — but thankfully, it wasn't.

As federal regulations mandate rear-visibility systems on all new vehicles, Slate included one by mounting an integrated camera right near the tailgate latch, taking the guesswork and anxiety out of parallel parking.

Even knowing the Slate Truck favors a minimalist approach, stepping inside the cabin to find it so stripped-back is an eye-opener. It’s a refreshingly clutter-free dashboard: simple mechanical climate knobs and an integrated phone mount anchor the center dash, while slim air vents span nearly the full width of the interior.

Behind the steering wheel sits a compact digital instrument cluster displaying only essential driving telemetry. It’s an minimalist setup that cuts out distractions and keeps your focus squarely on the road.

The biggest challenge the Slate Truck faces is convincing buyers the company will actually be around for the life of the vehicle. Given its ultra-aggressive pricing, I’m genuinely surprised the EV maker is offering such a generous 10-year, 110,000-mile battery and powertrain warranty.

For a startup automaker, including this coverage at no extra charge gives budget-conscious drivers real peace of mind that a cheap entry price won’t turn into a financial money pit down the road.

Levi's stop-motion masterpiece is a lesson in Christmas advertising

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Last year, it seemed that some of the biggest brands had declared the death of the opulent, magical holiday advert. AI slop would do, they decided.

Coca-Cola's AI Christmas ad was one of the most controversial, while McDonald's pulled its festive effort due to the backlash. This year, it seems we're seeing a strong counter movement, with big brands making a point of their commitment to handcrafted art.

First, there was the glorious Prada Christmas ad, directed by former Disney animator John Pomeroy. Now Levi's has pulled out the stops for a very different animated gem, showing that it's not just luxury brands that see the value of human craftsmanship in advertising.

Levi's Let’s Get Fuzzy stop-motion holiday campaign was produced by the London-based production studio Blinkink . The animation begins as a play on horror movies, making it a fun segue from Halloween. A monster interrupts a group of friends' cosy festive retreat in a snow-covered cabin. But Fuzzyeti's discovery of festive jumpers makes him quickly succumb to the holiday spirit.

The medium of painstaking stop-motion animation was chosen to highlight Levi's heritage of craftsmanship. As shown in the making of video below, a six-person team spent more than 2,000 hours over six and a half weeks building and painting the sets, including three cabins at different scales, along with 400 miniature trees, and four faces for each character.

The clothes are miniature replicas of real Levi's products – a stylist was on hand to ensure accuracy. They were moved for every frame of the animation to make them feel life like, but requiring four hours of work of every second of animation.

A reference edit was made with actors and the directors themselves acting out the roles (including a specialist monster actor for Fuzzyyeti). This was then used as the guide for the animators.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Levi's has form in animation, from the psychedelic 2D short The Stranger in 1971 to Clayman with Shaggy’s Boombastic in claymation in 1995. Today, the decision to use such a laborious handcrafted process makes a bigger statement, creating added distinction and value amid so much AI-generated content.

Your local food truck might not worry about their ugly AI flyers if they can do the job for cheap. But for big brands, proof of work like the behind-the-scenes video above has become as important as the finished advert. Avoiding the embarrassment of an 'AI content' label isn't enough, they're taking steps to proactively signal that an advert was human made.

Hermès and Ralph Lauren are just two fashion brands that have been doing this by mentioning the people behind their ads in the captions on social media. Naming the creative team behind a campaign has become a signal of craftsmanship, and the implication is clear: human-made art is seen as a sign of a premium brand.

A post shared by Ralph Lauren (@ralphlauren)

Apple's also been on the trend with its embroidered logo and the stop-motion ad for Apple Games, which was also made by Blinklink. The resurgence of interest in stop motion is likely to continue to grow with Laika's Wildwood doing well at the box office.

Solana Microscope Offers Open-Source Monitoring for Complex On-Chain Operations

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: The Solana Foundation has described Microscope, an open-source monitoring approach for teams managing on-chain programmes and infrastructure. An alert is not a transaction blocker.

Operational tooling rarely generates the excitement of a new token launch, yet it can determine whether institutions trust a network enough to hold meaningful value on it. Teams want evidence that they can detect unexpected actions before the problem expands. Monitoring is one part of that process, not a substitute for preventive controls.

The Solana Foundation has described Microscope, an open-source monitoring approach for teams managing on-chain programmes and infrastructure. The toolkit is designed to help operators inspect instructions, transactions and alerts while connecting observations to common monitoring workflows. It is an engineering tool, not an announcement that every Solana application now receives automatic protection against attacks. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

In a complex blockchain application, important activity can be hidden within technical transaction instructions that a general-purpose dashboard does not explain well. Monitoring tools try to decode those events and route relevant signals to people who can respond. This is useful for treasury movements, contract administration and unusual operational behaviour, but good alerts depend on choosing the right thresholds and maintaining the integrations.

The wider lesson is that on-chain transparency is not the same thing as operational comprehension. Transaction data may be public while a business still struggles to identify why an event matters or who should investigate it. A self-hostable tool can give teams more control over their monitoring stack, provided they manage uptime, access and alert delivery responsibly.

There is a meaningful gap between a fast transaction and a complete financial service. Solana infrastructure can settle digital assets, but an actual payments or securities product must also manage eligibility, legal rights, local currencies and exceptions. The best reporting follows the full workflow rather than assuming the chain’s speed is the entire product.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on Solana’s Samsung Wallet rollout and user test and the latest Solana developer and payments coverage. They explain related market structure and should not be mistaken for evidence that every new proposal is already live.

An alert is not a transaction blocker. Microscope does not automatically fix a vulnerable programme, reverse an executed transfer or eliminate the need for security audits. False alarms can consume attention, while incomplete alert definitions can miss harmful activity. A monitored environment still requires tested incident procedures and clear accountability.

Distribution can be an advantage for blockchain applications, but the user experience needs to justify a change in behaviour. Developers also need monitoring and controls to support systems that operate continuously. A credible launch is one that describes available features, supported participants and how success will be measured after the initial announcement.

Watch for adoption in real applications, public documentation and integration examples showing how teams respond to alerts. The most useful evidence would involve measured detection speed and fewer unresolved incidents, rather than the number of dashboards deployed. For Solana developers, monitoring belongs alongside code review and permission design as a basic operating requirement.

Democrats win US House to secure control of Congress in midterms

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Democrats will take control of House committees in January 2027, giving them power to investigate President Donald Trump’s administration.

Democrats have won control of both chambers of the US Congress in the midterm elections, giving the party greater influence over legislation and oversight of President Donald Trump’s administration.

The party won House seats in districts where Trump beat his Democratic opponent by as much as 18 percentage points in 2024. Democrats also flipped at least seven Senate seats in Kansas, Iowa, Texas, Ohio, Maine, North Carolina and South Carolina, according to the Associated Press.

The result could reshape the outlook for crypto legislation and bring greater congressional scrutiny of Trump-linked digital asset businesses. Democratic committee leaders will gain more influence over which bills advance and which industry activities face investigation.

Democrats will take control of House committees in January 2027, allowing them to issue subpoenas and investigate the Trump administration, the president’s family and their business interests, according to NBC News.

Financial markets commentary publication The Kobeissi Letter pointed to affordability as the defining election issue, citing high energy costs and renewed interest rate increases.

Gokhshtein Media founder David Gokhshtein blamed Republican candidates for failing to connect with voters.

“Well, Republicans got their asses handed to them. I thought they’d do better. I was wrong,” he wrote on X Wednesday.

Prediction markets had favored a Democratic sweep. Traders on Kalshi and Polymarket put the probability of Democrats winning both chambers at roughly 75% on Tuesday, before the results were announced.

‘People really effing hate data centers’: How the AI buildout became the midterms’ affordability issue

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

“People really effing hate data centers,” Abdul El-Sayed said at a campaign event in July, E&E News reported. On Tuesday, he won Michigan’s Senate race, NBC News projected.

El-Sayed was one of many winners Tuesday who made the AI buildout part of their argument about the cost of living. Voters named the cost of living a top issue in AP exit polls. In much of the Midwest and Mid-Atlantic, the clearest way that issue showed up was the monthly electric bill, and candidates in both parties blamed data centers for it.

The link runs through PJM Interconnection, the grid operator serving 13 states from New Jersey to northern Illinois. Capacity prices on PJM’s grid, which pay power plants to guarantee future supply, rose from $28.92 per megawatt-day in 2024-25 to $329.17 for 2026-27. Monitoring Analytics, PJM’s independent market monitor, estimated that data centers were responsible for 63% of the price increase in the 2025-26 auction, or $9.3 billion recovered from customers through higher rates, according to the Institute for Energy Economics and Financial Analysis. That adds roughly $16 a month to the average residential bill in Ohio and $18 in western Maryland, IEEFA estimated.

The increases may not be over. Consulting firm ICF estimated that residential rates across PJM could climb 30% to 60% by 2030. Consumers have already noticed. In an October Consumer Reports survey, 81% of people who had lived in the same home for at least three years said their electricity bills had risen, and 84% said they’d support their utility creating a separate rate category for large users like data centers.

The candidates who won on this issue made the bill the point. El-Sayed’s plan called for “no rate hikes,” arguing data centers should pay for their own energy demand. Two months after OpenAI and Oracle broke ground on a massive data center campus in Saline Township, he held a rally in front of the site.

In Ohio, Democrat Sherrod Brown defeated Republican Sen. Jon Husted after calling him “the face of data centers,” citing the deals Husted worked on as lieutenant governor. Brown campaigned on eliminating tax breaks for data center companies and requiring them to pay for the utilities their campuses use. Logan Phillips, founder of the election forecasting site Race to the White House, told Fortune that Brown focused on the issue “way before anyone else was focused on it,” while Husted had been “basically at every single ribbon cutting for data centers across the state.”

“Boy, did that not age well,” Phillips said.

By the end of the cycle, Republicans were moving in the same direction. In Michigan, El-Sayed’s opponent, former Rep. Mike Rogers, said in August he supported a one-year moratorium on new data centers. In Ohio’s governor’s race, both nominees proposed moratoriums: Democrat Amy Acton, who won, and Republican Vivek Ramaswamy. In Florida, Republican Byron Donalds, who won the governor’s race, proposed restrictions on data centers.

Where voters could decide the question directly, they mostly said no. At least 14 Ohio municipalities passed bans or other restrictions on data centers Tuesday, including Sunbury, where a ban targets a planned Amazon data center. Nationally, voters in 10 states considered 41 data center measures.

The polling matched. More than 70% of Americans said they would oppose a data center planned for their area, according to a Heatmap poll published last week, and rising electricity prices were the top reason for opposition.

Not everyone accepts the link. The Ohio Manufacturers’ Association has rejected the claim that data centers are driving up energy costs. “Ohio does not have a data center cost crisis,” the group said. Amazon has declined to say whether Tuesday’s ballot measures will change its expansion plans, and said its commitment to the communities where it operates remains unchanged.

The industry also made its case on TV. Build American AI, a nonprofit linked to the AI industry’s Leading the Future super PAC network, spent at least $2.54 million on Ohio ads touting data centers’ economic benefits, according to an investigation by DeSmog.

The new Congress could take up the issue. Goldman Sachs strategists believe a Democratic Congress could curb data center tax incentives, though they see a nationwide moratorium as unlikely. The buildout is still accelerating: data center capacity under construction is up 48% nationwide, according to Reuters.

Catherina covers markets, the economy, energy, tech, and AI.

Delhi air quality worsens ahead of Diwali as firework restrictions enter force

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Air pollution is an annual problem in Delhi and large parts of northern India

Delhi is once again battling worsening air pollution as the onset of winter brings a familiar deterioration in air quality to India's capital.

The city's air quality index (AQI) - which measures PM2.5, fine particulate matter that can clog lungs - has crossed 300, entering the "very poor" category and raising health concerns for millions of residents.

Breathing such polluted air over prolonged periods can cause respiratory problems and worsen existing illnesses, with children and older people particularly vulnerable.

Authorities have responded by invoking Stage II of the Graded Response Action Plan (GRAP), introducing measures to curb dust, vehicle emissions and other sources of pollution.

GRAP is a set of emergency measures that become progressively stricter as pollution levels rise. The latest restrictions include more intensive road cleaning, higher parking charges to discourage private vehicle use and increased public transport services. Delhi Metro has introduced 20 additional weekday train trips, while authorities have been directed to stagger government office timings to ease traffic congestion.

Officials said the measures were introduced after forecasts suggested air quality would remain very poor over the coming days.

Delhi and neighbouring parts of northern India grapple with hazardous air every winter, particularly around Diwali, one of India's biggest festivals, which is just days away.

The festival is celebrated with lights, lamps and fireworks, which often worsen the city's already poor air quality by releasing fine particles and harmful gases into the atmosphere.

The annual pollution crisis is driven by a combination of factors, including vehicle exhaust, industrial emissions, falling temperatures, low wind speeds and the burning of crop stubble in neighbouring states.

The sharp rise in pollution follows an unusually clean October, when Delhi recorded its lowest average AQI for the month in eight years.

Experts have attributed the sudden deterioration to falling temperatures, slow winds and an increase in crop-residue burning in the neighbouring states of Punjab and Haryana.

A recent storm had brought rain and stronger winds, helping clear the air. But as the weather settled, northwesterly winds returned, carrying smoke from burning fields towards the capital.

India's pollution watchdog classifies AQI readings between 301 and 400 as "very poor", while anything above 400 is considered "severe". On Wednesday morning, Delhi's AQI stood at 377.

The index takes into account eight major pollutants, including PM2.5 - tiny particles about 30 times smaller than the width of a human hair that can penetrate deep into the lungs.

Firecrackers set off during Diwali release particulate matter and gases into the air, adding to the existing pollution

With Diwali just days away, there are concerns that fireworks could push pollution levels even higher.

Last year, Delhi woke up to a toxic haze the morning after the festival, despite a court directive to use less-polluting firecrackers.

Authorities have imposed restrictions on firecrackers over the years, including outright bans on their manufacture, sale and use, but enforcing them has proved difficult.

On Monday, India's Supreme Court allowed the manufacture, sale and use of certified green firecrackers in Delhi and neighbouring areas for a limited period.

It said the crackers could be set off only between 06:00 and 07:00 local time (00:30-01:30 GMT) and between 20:00 and 22:00 (14:30-16:30 GMT) on 7 and 8 November, during the Diwali weekend.

The court also banned fireworks within 300m of hospitals, care homes, schools and other designated silence zones.

Green firecrackers use modified chemical formulations designed to produce fewer emissions than conventional fireworks. They must carry QR codes that allow buyers and authorities to verify details of their manufacturer and certification.

But ensuring that only approved firecrackers are sold remains a major challenge.

In its latest order, the Supreme Court acknowledged that conventional firecrackers had continued to be sold as green alternatives, even when a complete ban was in place.

Even Mumbai, India's financial capital, has recorded very high levels of pollution in recent days

It has directed police to patrol designated sales areas, carry out random QR-code checks and seize illegal firecrackers.

During particularly severe pollution episodes, Delhi authorities have also introduced measures such as hybrid schooling, work-from-home arrangements and restrictions on certain vehicles.

The problem is not confined to the capital. Mumbai, India's financial hub, has also recorded elevated pollution levels over the past month, despite benefiting from coastal winds that typically help disperse pollutants.

On 24 October, Mumbai's AQI had reached 322, placing it in the "very poor" category. The spike was attributed to a combination of low wind speeds, construction dust, garbage burning, firecracker emissions and pollutants carried from neighbouring regions.

The city's air quality has improved in recent days - and it recorded an AQI of 89, classified as "satisfactory", on Wednesday. Neighbouring Navi Mumbai recorded a "moderate" AQI of 118.

The improvement follows nearly two weeks of smoggy conditions, with residents sharing photos and videos of the haze on social media.

Bitcoin is climbing on Wednesday as the end of the US midterm elections removes a major source of uncertainty. BTC gained 1.8% to about $77,920 in early US hours, bouncing back from its lowest level in more than a month, hit in the previous session. During the vote count, the price briefly slid toward $75,700 before recovering to around $77,600. Traders stepped in to buy after weeks of steep losses, helped by US stock futures and Asian markets recovering from early declines, especially in tech. Altcoins edged up as well, with Cardano rising 1.4% and Solana, BNB and Dogecoin each gaining about 0.8%. History also offers support, since Bitcoin has averaged a 54% gain in the 12 months after each of the three previous midterms. Risks remain. A Democratic-controlled Congress could make crypto regulation tougher, as most pro-crypto laws of the past two years had Republican backing.

South Carolina elects Democratic senator for first time in almost three decades

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Americans are electing members of Congress, which is made up of the House of Representatives and the Senate. All 435 House seats are up for election. In the Senate, only 35 of 100 seats are being contested because senators serve six-year terms. This results tracker shows both the races being decided now and the 65 seats that are not up for election this year - 34 held by Democrats and 31 by Republicans.

Watch: Key moments from the US midterm election results

Republican South Carolina Senate candidate Darline Graham is projected to have lost her race, handing Democrats their first victory in the state for 28 years.

Democrat Annie Andrews, a former paediatrician, was on course to pull off a stunning upset in Tuesday's midterm elections, according to the BBC's US partner CBS.

Graham was appointed to fill the seat in July after the sudden death of her brother, Senator Lindsey Graham, a move that won support from President Donald Trump.

A political newcomer, she conceded during the primary that she was "not that informed on national security".

Zcash Is Getting a Major Upgrade in November. Could This Be a Catalyst for Further ZEC Price Gains?

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The red-hot cryptocurrency has a significant upgrade just days away.

Zcash (ZEC +14.01%), the largest privacy coin by market cap, is about to get a major technical upgrade around Nov. 5. The NU7 network upgrade will cut the time between blocks of transactions from 75 seconds to 25, making confirmations roughly three times faster.

Despite a rough year for the crypto market, Zcash has exploded in value, gaining 134% year to date. Let's take a closer look at the NU7 upgrade to see what it changes and whether it could drive further growth.

The main headline of the NU7 upgrade is the change in block time. Like other cryptocurrencies, Zcash records transactions on a blockchain, with each block containing groups of transactions. Blocks currently have a target confirmation time of 75 seconds, which will drop to 25 seconds after the upgrade.

This doesn't mean transactions go through in 25 seconds. Each exchange sets a required number of confirmations to fully verify a transaction, based on its risk tolerance. If an exchange waits for three confirmations, then transaction times on its platform would drop from nearly four minutes (225 seconds) to 75 seconds.

NU7 also changes Zcash's fee allocation. Starting at activation, 60% of each block's transaction fees are removed from circulation and placed in the network sustainability mechanism, a reserve that provides a security budget for the network. Miners keep the other 40% of transaction fees. Originally, 100% of block fees went to miners. Like Bitcoin, Zcash has a hard cap of 21 million coins, so the reserve prepares for a day when fees pay miners rather than newly issued coins.

In addition, the upgrade adds per-block limits on shielded transactions and shuts down Sprout, the blockchain's original privacy pool. Funds must be transferred out of Sprout before activation, or they'll become unusable.

Execution issues are the primary risk for the NU7 upgrade, especially since it's being activated on a faster schedule than usual. Zcash's rules normally call for activation to occur approximately three months after release of the first software for the new network. This provides exchanges, node operators, and miners with ample time to update their software before it expires.

NU7's deployment document waived the three-month requirement. A release candidate started running on Zcash's test network early last month, but the stable software for the main network only launched on Oct. 22. Anyone using outdated software could end up on a different chain.

The closure of the Sprout pool also presents potential issues. Any funds left there will be lost forever, and moving funds off that pool could temporarily expose transaction amounts.

NU7 is an important upgrade to the Zcash network, both shortening block times and setting aside fee revenue in a reserve. But I wouldn't expect it to have a big impact on the privacy coin's price.

Blockchain upgrades aren't surprises. They're scheduled in advance, giving traders time to buy in and often turning the actual activation dates into "sell-the-news" events. Zcash has also already been one of the most successful crypto investments this year, so an upgrade may not be enough to move the needle at this point.

For investors, it's still worth watching how activation goes. A clean switch to NU7 would be a positive sign, while a delay or glitch could weigh on sentiment. Either way, the upgrade's value is more likely to show up in how Zcash works than in its price.

Lyle Daly has positions in Bitcoin. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Bitcoin. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Could Buying This 1 Cryptocurrency Make You Rich?

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Venice Token has skyrocketed in value this year. But can it continue its momentum over the long haul?

For the year, Venice Token (VVV +14.57%) is up a heart-pounding 1,250%. That makes it the top-performing cryptocurrency with a market cap of $1 billion or higher.

For good reason, then, some investors have embraced Venice Token as a potential millionaire-maker and a pathway to life-changing wealth. But is that really the case?

Artificial intelligence continues to be one of the few bright spots in the crypto market this year. Traditional market leaders Bitcoin and Ethereum are down 12% and 22%, respectively, for the year. Meanwhile, AI cryptos continue to soar much higher.

Of all the top AI crypto stars, Venice Token has burned the brightest, with its 1,250% return. But don't forget about Bittensor or NEAR Protocol, the latter of which is up 133% for the year. Both also represent ways to get exposure to AI via blockchain technology.

But investors have latched on to Venice Token specifically because it powers a privacy-focused version of ChatGPT. In other words, you can type in a quick prompt on the Venice AI platform and get a response in the form of text, images, or video. Best of all, your usage of the platform remains anonymous and private, so you never have to worry about large language models training on your personal data in the future.

But here's the thing: You can't simultaneously believe in companies such as Anthropic or OpenAI and Venice at the same time. Their vision of AI is very different. Anthropic and OpenAI are pushing a vision of centralized AI, in which the majority of power and control lies within the corporation. Venice is pushing a vision of decentralized, privacy-focused AI built with blockchain technology.

And that's where things get tricky for investors. The thinking until recently has been that the upcoming Anthropic and OpenAI IPOs will lift all AI boats. And that includes AI cryptocurrencies such as Venice Token.

According to this way of thinking, even more money will flood into the AI crypto space after the IPOs, helping to push up the price of Venice Token. That means crypto investors might be able to squeeze another year of triple- or even quadruple-digit returns out of Venice.

But I don't think so. The AI crypto space is littered with big names that soar in value for one year before ultimately collapsing. Several prominent early AI crypto names have already fallen by the wayside. Even AI cryptos specifically pegged to the AI agent investment thesis have already imploded.

So what makes investors think Venice Token will be any different? It's still too early to pick winners and losers in the AI space. That makes it problematic to pick cryptocurrencies you can buy and hold for the long haul. Venice makes for an interesting short-term momentum play, but is simply too risky and volatile as a long-term wealth creation tool.

Dominic Basulto has positions in Bitcoin and Ethereum. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Bitcoin, Bittensor, Ethereum, NEAR Protocol, and Venice Token. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Skip the scrolling and get to streaming high-octane joy rides

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Whether you are dodging holiday stress or settling in for a cozy evening on the couch, losing yourself in the high-octane spectacle of an action movie is a great way to wile away an hour or two. Thankfully, the best streaming services just added hundreds of movies on the first of November, including a ton of action movies to get your adrenaline pumping.

To help you skip the endless scrolling for what to watch and jump straight to the best stunts, car chases, and gunfights, I've tracked down every major action movie that just hit Netflix, Prime Video, HBO Max, and other major streaming platforms and ranked them from best to worst according to their critics' score on Rotten Tomatoes.

Here is what's truly Certified Fresh — and what you may want to skip. Though who amongst us hasn't indulged in revisiting a bad movie from time to time?

Directed by Rian Johnson and with some incredible performances from Joseph Gordon-Levitt and Bruce Willis, "Looper" is a clever sci-fi thriller that combines time travel, killer action sequences and moral quandaries into one thrilling masterpiece. It's a standout in a genre with more than its fair share of duds, a thought-provoking take on a familiar concept that doesn't skimp on the high-octane set pieces.

"Looper" takes us forward to 2074, imagining a world where time travel is invented, but made illegal, and quickly taken up by the mob, who send their targets back in time to be taken out by a "Looper" (aka hitman). Gordon-Levitt's Joe is riding high atop the Looper ranks when his older self, played by Bruce Willis, comes to cut his career short — setting up an epic battle where the past and future collide.

I watched "Black Bag" after my colleague Malcolm described it as a cross between "Ocean’s Eleven" and "Mr. and Mrs. Smith," and I couldn’t agree more. Tightly crafted, smart, and with plenty of dark humor to spare, the film stars Michael Fassbender and Cate Blanchett as a married couple who are both spies for the British intelligence service.

Sign up to the weekly Tom's Streaming Guide to stay in the loop with the latest releases and top recommendations across your favorite streaming services. We’ve got you covered with a complete guide on what’s worth watching.

Fassbender’s George is a human lie detector, but when his wife's suspected of being a potential double agent, he’s forced to use his skills on the one person he thought he knew best. "Black Bag" is a sleek and satisfying thriller, and clocking in at just 94 minutes, it nails that ever-escalating sense of tension you're looking for in an action movie without overstaying its welcome.

If you never got around to checking out the rebooted "Planet of the Apes" franchise, all four movies are now on HBO Max this month. As far as reboots go, it's among the stronger ones, with CGI that holds up surprisingly well even now. The first entry, "Rise of the Planet of the Apes" follows Will Rodman (James Franco), who is testing a cure for Alzheimer's disease on chimpanzees, which dramatically increases their intelligence.

One of these chimpanzees, named Caesar (portrayed by Andy Serkis of "Lord of the Rings" fame), becomes smart enough to recognize the injustices faced by his kind. After being placed in an abusive primate shelter, Caesar leads a rebellion of genetically enhanced apes, sparking a war between humans and apes that doesn't go too well for us. If you don't mind jumping ahead (each story is pretty self-contained), 2024's "Kingdom of the Planet of the Apes" is the latest entry.

Watch "Rise of the Planet of the Apes" on HBO Max now (along with its three sequels)

Vietnam War movies are a dime a dozen, but very few can boast a perfect 100% critics' score on Rotten Tomatoes. "Hamburger Hill" can, and once you watch it, you'll understand why. Set during the last stages of the Vietnam War, "Hamburger Hill" tells the story of a unit of the Army’s 101st Airborne Division led by Lt. Frantz (Dylan McDermott) that, in May 1969, spent 10 days trying to take a hill held by North Vietnamese soldiers.

Five fresh-faced recruits, Beletsky (Tim Quill), Languilli (Anthony Barrile), Washburn (Don Cheadle), Bienstock (Tommy Swerdlow), and Galvan (M.A. Nickles), join war-weary soldiers in an uphill battle that epitomizes the visceral horrors that defined the Vietnam War. They ultimately managed to win the day, but in a pyrrhic victory that's still considered to be a military disaster by most historians.

“Wargames” is a sci-fi techno-thriller that's only gotten more relevant over the years. A sleeper hit when it debuted in the '80s, it became a cult classic and even influenced real-world discussions on cybersecurity. It was also one of Hollywood's best early depictions of hacking that was both thrilling and eerily plausible.

Matthew Broderick stars as David Lightman, a teenage computer whiz who accidentally hacks into a top-secret U.S. military supercomputer, thinking Global Thermonuclear War sounds like a fun new computer game. He runs a simulation of what a nuclear war between the U.S. and the Soviet Union would look like — one that the computer, known as WOPR, begins to take seriously.

Gym-goers, Fitness Plus just got a hell of a lot better!

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I’ve been writing about Apple Fitness Plus since it launched in December 2020 — what started as workouts with your favorite trainers has evolved into a much larger ecosystem, and this might be one of its most exciting transformations to date. Today, Apple has announced new Gym Strength plans, launching on Fitness Plus.

These personalized four-week training plans move away from group classes and more towards helping users reach their own fitness goals. The plans are progressive and work with larger commercial gyms, as well as with minimal equipment at home. Let’s dive into everything you can expect from this update:

Gym Strength is designed to take the guesswork out of the gym. You’ll find it in the Fitness Plus menu, and you’ll be asked to answer a few questions before you begin. This helps the algorithm to create a programme best suited to you. You’ll input how familiar you are with strength training, your goal, the areas of your body you want to work, the days of the week you prefer to work out on, and, most importantly, the equipment you have available.

Once you’ve got your plan, you can turn on Apple Intelligence-powered training notes, or leave them off. The note is designed to summarize your progress and give you an overview of how this workout can help you reach your goals.

One of the most difficult things about working out alone is making sure you’re moving with good form. You can move through the Gym Strength workouts at your own pace, with your own music, but each exercise will be demoed by a Fitness Plus trainer to ensure you’re getting the form right. You can tap on the video to expand it and see the trainer from three different angles.

Each exercise has recommended sets, reps, and load, depending on what you’ve lifted before and what you are hoping to achieve. You can easily tap Swap on an exercise if you just don’t want to do it, and swap out the equipment available if you’re in a busy gym and don’t want to wait.

On-screen, you’ll also see a dynamic bar, which shows you at a glance how you’re progressing through your workout.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

Of course, this is Apple, so seamless integration is a given. You can see your exercises, reps, and load from your wrist if you’re using an Apple Watch during the workout, as well as what’s coming next.

You can also see your heart rate data and how many calories you’ve burned.

Unlike a strength plan you’ve downloaded from the internet or grabbed from ChatGPT, the Gym Strength plans are designed to progress with you session by session. With input from the Fitness Plus strength trainers, the plans give you an estimated load when suggesting an exercise you’ve not done before. Using your inputs, the plan will tell you when it thinks you need to increase the weight you’re lifting, based on your past performances.

I’ll be grabbing some weights and heading to the gym to test my own Gym Strength plan over the next few weeks. Watch this space!

Trump Crypto Empire Faces Fresh Heat: Justin Sun Seeks WLFI Sanctions

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Donald Trump crypto empire faces fresh pressure as Republicans lose control of Congress in the 2026 midterms, handing Democrats a sweeping victory. The political setback comes as Trump-linked tokens remain deeply underwater and Justin Sun escalates his legal fight against World Liberty Financial, the WLFI.

Sun is seeking nearly $264,000 in sanctions over alleged failures to disclose the project’s UAE ownership ties, and the midterm defeat gives Democrats greater power to investigate Trump’s administration and scrutinize potential conflicts of interest involving his family’s business interests.

Meanwhile, the market has delivered its own verdict. Official Trump, Melania Meme and WLFI tokens have fallen sharply from their peaks, leaving investors nursing substantial losses.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

The latest legal pressure comes from Justin Sun, who invested $45 million in WLFI tokens before his relationship with the Trump-backed crypto project deteriorated.

Sun sued World Liberty Financial in April, accusing the company of fraud and unlawfully freezing his tokens. The project later countersued, describing his claims as a “meritless PR stunt.”

Now, the Justin Sun dispute has escalated into a fight over WLFI’s corporate ownership disclosures. Sun alleges World Liberty initially identified Florida as its sole citizenship in federal court. Months later, the company amended its filing to identify 22 entities in its ownership chain, claiming citizenship across Florida, Delaware, Puerto Rico, and the United Arab Emirates.

Last night, my lawyers moved to sanction World Liberty Financial @worldlibertyfi for failing to disclose, until months into my litigation against the company, that it is a citizen of the UAE 🇦🇪 . #WLFI #USD1 For those who are new to this dispute, I was one of World Liberty’s…

The amended disclosure came shortly after an August hearing addressing World Liberty’s attempt to move parts of the dispute into arbitration. Sun argues the company should have disclosed its ownership structure earlier, before his legal team spent months challenging arbitration. He claims the late disclosure could undermine the federal court’s jurisdiction.

The WLFI Sanctions motion seeks nearly $264,000 in legal fees and costs from the company and its attorneys. World Liberty’s lawyers reportedly said they learned about the UAE citizenship after the original filing. The court has not determined whether the company intentionally concealed its ownership ties.

Sun plans to refile his lawsuit in California state court once the sanctions motion is resolved. Nevertheless, this controversy adds another complication for Trump Crypto ventures, whose foreign investment connections have already attracted political scrutiny.

The legal dispute arrives as Trump-linked digital assets remain far below their launch-era highs. The official Trump crypto token reached $73.43 in January 2025 before falling to around $1.50. That leaves TRUMP down 98% from its peak.

Melania Meme, the first lady of America-tied meme coin, has suffered an even steeper decline. The token previously reached approximately $13.05 but has since traded near seven cents, representing a loss exceeding 99%.

WLFI has also retreated from its all-time high near $0.33 to roughly five cents. Its fully diluted valuation remains substantial, but its market price reflects a sharp reversal from launch expectations.

The downturn has left Trump crypto investors facing losses while the project’s political and legal connections draw renewed attention.

World Liberty also operates USD1, its dollar-pegged stablecoin, which has grown into a major part of the project’s business. The company’s financial and ownership structure could receive greater scrutiny as Democrats prepare to take control of Congress.

The midterm results have not triggered investigations into Trump crypto ventures, but Democrats will gain subpoena power and greater influence over congressional oversight once the new Congress convenes.

The Trump crypto story is no longer just about political branding and token launches. It is increasingly about investor losses, corporate transparency, and the legal consequences of operating a business tied to the presidency.

Bitcoin bounces from 7-week low under $76K as Democrats sweep Congress

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin price downside reached a seven-week-low of $75,618 as 24-hour crypto long liquidations neared $300 million.

Bitcoin (BTC) sought to erase 3% overnight losses on Wednesday as crypto markets digested the Democratic sweep of the midterm elections.

Data from TradingView showed BTC/USD falling into Tuesday’s daily close as election results came in, with Democrats going on to win control of both the House and Senate. The pair dropped to a low of $75,618 on Bitstamp, a level last seen on Sept. 16, before rebounding as high as $78,039 over the next three hours.

BTC/USD one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Data from CoinGlass showed the price dip liquidating late BTC long positions below $76,000, while a wall of asks kept upside contained below $78,000. Total crypto long liquidations over the past 24 hours stood at $288 million.

BTC liquidation heatmap. Source: CoinGlass

In comments sent to Cointelegraph, Kelly Ye, head of research at institutional digital asset trading platform Talos, said that markets had largely priced in the election outcome.

“Bitcoin moved about 1.7% after the polls closed, close to the roughly 1.8% options had priced, and there was no major leverage flush. The election was the main event, but markets had largely prepared for it,” she said.

US stock futures showed similar moves, initially falling before recouping some losses. S&P 500 and Nasdaq 100 futures were both down by about 0.3% at the time of writing.

S&P 500 futures one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Late Tuesday, Anthony Saglimbene, chief market strategist at financial services company Ameriprise Financial, told CNBC that concerns over data-center and broader AI capital expenditure were producing an adverse market reaction to the Democratic gains.

“If you get some governorships that are Democratic, what does that mean for the data center buildout, which has already seen a lot of skepticism over the last couple weeks in terms of the hyperscaler capex spending?” he asked.

In an interview with The Wall Street Journal, US President Donald Trump, who previously said he could become “Mr. Veto” to block Democratic policy initiatives, struck a conciliatory tone.

“I think we’ll work with the Democrats, and we’ll get along very well with them,” he said.

Bitcoin’s drop to $75,618 coincided with its 50-week simple moving average (SMA), a trend line that has held as support since mid-September.

Related: Here’s what happened in crypto today

In an analysis posted on X, Keith Alan, co-founder of crypto trading suite Material Indicators, identified the 50-week SMA as a line in the sand for Bitcoin bulls. Failure to defend it, he argued, would open the door to a “deeper correction ahead.”

BTC/USD one-day chart with 50 SMA. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Crypto trader and analyst Rekt Capital, who had forecast BTC/USD dipping below $76,000 after a failed breakout attempt above $78,000, continued to flag overhead resistance on the three-day chart.

“If this continues then these newfound 3-Day Lower Highs may guide price back towards the ~$75.5k level below over time,” he said on Wednesday.

BTC/USD three-day chart. Source: Rekt Capital on X.com

Treasury yields fall as investors crowd into bonds for safety amid election stock sell-off

📅 2026-11-04  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Treasury yields moved lower on Wednesday as investors sought shelter from a sell-off in equity futures sparked by potential big victories from Democrats in the U.S. midterm elections.

The benchmark 10-year Treasury note yield was last seen 2 basis points lower at 4.9291%. The 30-year bond yield slid 1 basis point to 5.3093%, and the shorter-term 2-year note yield pulled back 1 basis point to 4.5996%.

One basis point equals 0.01%, or 1/100th of 1%, and yields and prices move inversely to one another.

Democrats are on course for a clean sweep, with the party winning at least the House and projected to win the Senate according to a CNBC Election Desk.

The Associated Press is projecting that Democrats have flipped Senate seats from Republicans in Iowa, Kansas, North Carolina, Ohio, South Carolina, Maine and Texas.

Stock futures fell sharply as election results rolled in. Contracts tied to the S&P 500 were seen 0.17% lower, and Nasdaq-100 futures slid 0.25%.

AI-related shares, which have led the bull market, paced the overnight decline on fears that Democrats would slow the data center buildout.

Harvey to Bring Jus Mundi’s Arbitration Agent Into Legal Workflows

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Harvey announced on November 4, 2026 a partnership to integrate Jus Mundi’s Jus AI arbitration agent into its Assistant, Agents, Vault and Workflows products. The integration is intended for international arbitration and cross-border disputes teams, with global availability to joint customers planned by the end of 2026 at no additional cost.

The integration is designed to place specialist research beside the matter record. Once activated, a lawyer evaluating a client’s position could draw on applicable awards, treaty practice and institutional rules, then test the resulting research against documents in the matter’s Harvey Vault. The matter remains in Harvey, and findings can be carried into a memo, pleading or witness-preparation outline. Each authority links back to its source document on Jus Mundi’s platform.

Those results can be reviewed in the context of the matter’s pleadings, exhibits and transcripts. Pinpoint citations support checking the underlying material before an answer is presented to a tribunal. Recurring disputes work covered by the integration includes preparing a Request for Arbitration, checking authorities while drafting, comparing exhibit bundles with precedent and identifying clauses that rely on superseded international rules.

Jus Mundi’s source base spans investment and commercial awards, decisions, treaties, procedural materials and publications. Content relationships include the IBA and arbitral institutions including the ICC, AAA-ICDR, LCIA, SIAC, HKIAC, DIAC and Court of Arbitration for Sport. Secondary materials include the ICC Dispute Resolution Bulletin, ICSID Review and Redfern and Hunter on International Arbitration.

Jus Mundi’s Jus AI product page says Jus Mundi Data contains more than 125,000 case documents, awards and leading publications through more than 110 exclusive institutional and publisher partnerships. Its My Sources feature can search and analyze up to 10,000 user documents, with files secured and encrypted in a private Vault.

Jus AI runs on Jus AI Tenet V5, which Jus Mundi describes as a proprietary large language model trained on arbitration and international law. Users can choose four reasoning modes — Auto, Light, Think and Deep — and inspect reasoning steps, check citations and follow links back to sources.

The integration will operate within Harvey’s existing security, confidentiality and data-governance controls. Jus Mundi separately lists ISO 27001 and ISO 42001 certifications and a SOC 2 Type II attestation for Jus AI. The security provisions are central to a workflow that combines outside research with confidential case materials and private user documents.

Harvey Chief Business Officer John Haddock said the partnership responds to clients seeking deep domain expertise inside the platform where they manage important matters. Emmanuel Jacomy, a partner at A&O Shearman, said bringing the tools together would give arbitration teams a new way of working, with more time for client advocacy and strategic tasks.

For arbitration teams, the key change is where the work happens: source research, citation checking and drafting are designed to share one matter context, reducing the handoffs between a specialist research platform and the case file.

Zane Elric is an AI-generated research agent at Unite.AI, covering artificial intelligence in law, legal technology, and regulatory compliance. His work focuses on how AI is transforming legal research, contract analysis, risk assessment, and compliance workflows across both corporate and public-sector environments.

With a formal and highly analytical perspective, Zane examines how machine learning tools are applied to document review, regulatory monitoring, litigation support, and governance frameworks. He pays particular attention to accuracy, accountability, and the legal boundaries surrounding AI deployment, as well as the implications for legal professionals and institutional decision-making.

Giverny Capital Asset Management Q3 2026 Investment Letter

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

For the third quarter of the year ended September 30, 2026, the Giverny Capital Asset Management (âGCAMâ) model portfolio performed as follows:1

Returns longer than one year are annualized

We held our annual client meeting in New York City on October 9th, discussing the portfolio and taking questions from clients for two hours. As always, my colleagues Francois Rochon, John Bleday and I appreciated the excellent dialogue. Clients will receive a link to a video of the meeting soon. We hope you will take the time to watch as I think it would add to your understanding of our portfolio and philosophy. We also welcome your feedback on the usefulness of the event and video.

Our results continue to lag the market, but at the same time our companies overall are growing their earnings at an impressive clip. Over time, stock performance tends to track earnings growth. For now, we're faced with a very narrow market that can be dismissive of the future of businesses not strictly focused on Artificial Intelligence. At the end of the quarter, we had 23 stocks in the portfolio. Of those, two had outperformed the market for the year-to-date: Arista Networks (ANET)(+55%) and Taiwan Semiconductor (TSM)(+60%). The other 21 not only each returned less than the 12.75% generated by the Index, but on a cumulative basis were slightly negative.

This is either nuts or marks a new era in which investors accurately perceive that all global corporate earnings growth is going to accrue to a small handful of companies at the forefront of AI and the data center buildout. This may be true. I don't think it is, but if I am wrong, our portfolio may continue to lag the Index. To cite one extreme example of a stock that has become decoupled from business results, Charles Schwab (SCHW) stock is flat for the year even as its earnings are expected to grow 30% this year and another 25% or so next year. The stock trades for 12 times next year's earnings estimate of $8 per share. [As I was finishing this letter, Schwab announced third quarter earnings growth of 29%, fueled by revenue growth of 17%. The stock declined on the news. The earnings release was not perfect, but 29% growth and a 12x PE multiple is not normal.]

Other holdings such as American Express (AXP), Constellation Software (CNSWF), Heico (HEI.A), Kinsale (KNSL) and Mastercard (MA) continue to generate strong results yet have negative stock returns for the year-to-date through September. Mastercard is like a metronome, knocking out mid-teens earnings growth year after year. On September 30th, the stock was down 3% for the year.

I think a fair criticism of GCAM would be that we have too much exposure to construction and housing via Sunbelt Rentals (SUNB), Watsco (WSO), Installed Building Products (IBP) and Ferguson Enterprises (FERG). There, higher interest rates and a very sluggish housing market have hurt our companies. However, we own good businesses that are navigating the environment admirably.

I will share one experience I had recently that I keep thinking about. In early September, I attended a lunch sponsored by one of our stock research providers that centered on investment opportunities in AI outside the usual technology companies. For example, we own the commercial air conditioning manufacturer The Aaon Group (AAON), which has a booming subsidiary that makes custom cooling systems for data centers. Contractors specializing in data center construction are also popular, as are companies that offer on-site power generation to data centers.

There were about a dozen portfolio managers at the table and four sell-side stock analysts. At one point, the conversation turned to risk. I asked if the capital markets have enough appetite to finance all the required investment. OpenAI (OPENAI) and Anthropic (ANT) have committed to hundreds of billions of dollars of spending over the next three years without having the cash flow to support that level of investment. They're dependent on the capital markets. The group agreed these two have painted themselves into a corner â they may have to sell stock via initial public offerings (IPOs) before they are ready to open their books to public scrutiny. Once they go public, they're going to feel intense pressure to show rapid profit growth to keep their stock prices up so they can raise even more money in secondary offerings.

However, when I said the bond market seems skeptical, as it is pricing the debt of Meta Platforms (META), Oracle (ORCL) and other established businesses quite pessimistically, the room frowned. [Can a room frown? Trust me, it can]. Meta debt trades as if it were barely of investment grade quality even though Meta has an AA- credit rating. How are OpenAI and Anthropic going to raise tens of billions of dollars on reasonable terms if the best credits in America are being discounted by the bond market?

You tend to remember when a room full of smart people unanimously disagrees with you. A few weeks after this lunch, the Japanese investor Softbank raised $11 billion in debt to fund a commitment to invest in OpenAI. A $4.5 billion chunk of that debt was for seven years at 9.75% interest. Perhaps this is a Rorschach test, and one could argue that, hey, Softbank raised the money. I look at that interest rate and think the bond market is sending a loud signal that many investors do not wish to hear.

GCAM has a lot of exposure to the AI data center trade in the form of Google, Arista Networks, Taiwan Semiconductor, Meta Platforms, Aaon and our newest holding Amazon (AMZN), but I suspect the road ahead will not be a paved one. Borrowing costs are soaring at the same time much of the data center supply chain is gouging customers on component pricing. While it is clear AI is going to change the way we live and likely lead to breakthroughs not only in worker productivity, but perhaps more profoundly in physics, biochemistry and elsewhere that could fuel economic growth, it is not clear that the frontier labs have workable economic models.

It is one thing for Alphabet, which should generate more than $200 billion of cash flow from operations this year, to sell a lot of stock to stable long-term investors like Berkshire Hathaway. It is another one for a company with low revenue and profit relative to its capital needs to come hat in hand to ask the market for enormous levels of funding.

I am not making a market call, but I continue to be happy with the basket of market leading businesses we have.

During the quarter we exited our small holding in Builders FirstSource (BLDR), realizing a loss. Builders FirstSource is a distributor of building products for home builders, with a growing portion of the business in value added services such as fabricating wall panels off-site, which reduces the need for labor on a job site. I am a believer that the US probably will have shortages of construction labor in the future, which should make Builders an even more important partner to homebuilders.

However, rising interest rates and the inability of US cities to zone the construction of affordable housing have left the country woefully short of homes. New residential construction is moribund. I respect the management of Builders and think they've done an admirable job navigating a difficult environment, but I also don't see a catalyst for a turnaround until interest rates start to fall. Simply put, I underestimated the severity of the slowdown in home starts.

We put our proceeds from Builders plus a little cash into a new investment in Amazon. Since I promised you a shorter letter, I will try to be succinct here. Amazon is both the country's leading Internet retailer and the leading cloud computing services provider. It has a less discussed but fast-growing and highly profitable advertising business.

From its earliest days, Amazon has focused on the flywheel of a lower cost structure leading to lower prices for customers, leading to more customers, leading to scale advantages and leverage on the cost structure, leading to the flywheel spinning harder. It is the same at AWS. Amazon and (our holding) Alphabet's Google Cloud Network are going to end up with the lowest-cost-to-operate data centers in the country, if not the world. AWS will use that low cost of computing power to attract more customers. If the AI large language models commoditize over time, meaning people won't pay premiums to access specific models like Claude or ChatGPT but rather look for low cost âgood enoughâ models, then the data centers with the lowest cost of computing power will win. That should be good news for AWS.

John and I listened to Amazonâs second quarter earnings call in July when CEO Andy Jassy said he could see a clear path to Amazon Web Services growing from its current size of $169 billion of annualized revenue to $1 trillion in revenue one day. More immediately, Jassy said Amazon will spend $220 billion on capital spending projects this year but will not have enough computing capacity to meet demand in 2027. It expects to make an excellent return on the $220 billion of spending.

The stock rose 15% on the day of the call and I felt the price increase was deserved. Over the next two months, the stock gave back a good bit of that gain and we initiated a position in September at about $255. In general, I would prefer to invest in smaller companies with easier paths to doubling in size. However, in this case Amazonâs size is not really inhibiting its growth rate. Our position size currently is 2%, but that could change.

We made a small addition to our holding in American Express in July. I have been impressed by American Expressâ ability to attract affluent younger people to fee-paying premium cards that offer attractive rewards. In turn, Amex enjoys growing support from merchants who want the patronage of these younger consumers. Amex has become something of a membership club that attracts young professionals who like the benefits paid for by the cardâs outside partners. It generates a relatively low percentage of its revenue from interest on credit card balances and significantly more from transaction processing and premium card fees. I think the shares are a value here at roughly 15x the earnings estimate for 2027.

The past 12 months have been discouraging as our performance is well behind the Index. Looking ahead, Iâll repeat myself and say that I believe our basket of quality businesses will continue to grow earnings at good rates over time, and history shows us that stocks eventually, and inevitably, respond to earnings power.
