US midterms election 2026 live: Democratic Senate odds climb above 90%

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Follow live coverage of the 2026 US midterm elections, including key congressional races, crypto-backed candidates and what the results could mean for digital asset regulation.

Within the next few hours, we will begin to learn whether President Donald Trump’s Republican Party has managed to retain control of the US Congress, determining his ability to advance his legislative agenda during the final two years of his term.

Follow the latest developments in congressional races attracting crypto industry spending, election results and reactions from prediction markets. Cointelegraph is using live election results data from the Associated Press. Updates appear in reverse chronological order.

Prediction markets increasingly favored Democrats taking control of both chambers of Congress on Tuesday evening.

As of 8:05 pm ET, the Blockchain Association’s election dashboard showed Democratic Senate-control odds at 94% on Kalshi and 93% on Polymarket, up from 75% and 75.5%, respectively, earlier in the day. Democratic House-control odds stood at 98% and 99%.

It comes as Democrats and allied independents have secured 42 Senate seats to Republicans’ 31.

Massachusetts Senator Ed Markey and Delaware Senator Chris Coons, both Democrats, win reelection, according to the Associated Press.

Markey voted against the GENIUS stablecoin bill in 2025, while Coons has criticized President Donald Trump’s crypto ventures, calling his latest memecoin-holder event “corruption.”

Democratic Sen. Jon Ossoff has won reelection in Georgia, beating Republican Representative Mike Collins.

Ossoff expressed support for impeaching Trump in April 2025 over plans to host a private dinner for top holders of the president’s TRUMP memecoin, arguing that the arrangement amounted to selling access to the presidency.

Maylea Ma, deputy general counsel at 1inch, said she expects federal agencies to drive the next phase of crypto regulation.

The election could reshape the path forward for the stalled CLARITY Act, but Ma said “real movement” in US digital-asset regulation over the next few years will come from the US Securities and Exchange Commission, Commodity Futures Trading Commission and Treasury, rather than Congress.

Ma said those agencies were increasingly engaging with crypto projects. However, agency engagement does not provide the stability the industry had hoped to secure through legislation.

“The long-term goal, of course, remains a statute that cannot be reversed every time a government changes,” said Ma.

Democratic Rep. Debbie Wasserman Schultz wins Florida’s 20th Congressional District. Wasserman Schultz voted against the CLARITY crypto market structure bill when it passed the House in July 2025.

Democratic Senator Mark Warner has won re-election in Virginia, defeating Republican challenger Bert Mizusawa. Warner was widely favored to win.

Warner voted against advancing the CLARITY Act in September, arguing that crypto legislation needs safeguards against President Donald Trump personally benefiting from the industry. Warner supports establishing federal rules for digital assets, with his opposition to the bill focused on its ethics protections.

The first polls in the US midterm elections closed at 6 pm ET in parts of Indiana and Kentucky. The remaining polls in both states close at 7 pm ET.

Polls also close at 7 pm ET in Georgia and Virginia, followed by Ohio at 7:30 pm ET, where Republican Sen. Jon Husted faces Democrat Sherrod Brown in a race drawing major crypto industry spending.

More than 57 million Americans had cast ballots as of Monday, with early voting surpassing 2022 levels in several closely watched states, according to the Associated Press.

In Ohio, Republican Sen. Jon Husted faces Democrat Sherrod Brown, a longtime crypto critic, in a race that has attracted approximately $63 million in crypto industry spending. That includes about $56 million from Fairshake and $7 million from the Winklevoss-backed Digital Freedom Fund, with both groups spending to support Husted or oppose Brown.

Crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto characterizes Brown as strongly opposed to cryptocurrency, citing 17 statements and his 2024 vote against overturning the SEC’s SAB 121 crypto custody accounting guidance.

Brown was also targeted by crypto industry spending in 2024, when Fairshake affiliate Defend American Jobs spent more than $40 million supporting Republican Bernie Moreno, who defeated Brown in that year’s Senate election.

Republican Sen. Pete Ricketts faces independent challenger Dan Osborn in Nebraska, where crypto political action committee Fairshake had spent nearly $11 million supporting Ricketts, according to an Oct. 27 report by the Washington Examiner.

More recent spending data shows Fairshake-affiliated crypto super PAC Defend American Jobs spent approximately $13.5 million opposing Osborn, according to Federal Election Commission data compiled by Bankrolled as of Nov. 3.

Ricketts, a member of the Senate Banking Committee, has supported cryptocurrency legislation including the CLARITY Act and GENIUS Act. He voted to advance the CLARITY Act out of committee in May and supported an unsuccessful Senate procedural vote on the legislation in September, according to crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto.

In Texas, Republican Ken Paxton faces Democrat James Talarico, with the Winklevoss-backed Digital Freedom Fund supporting Paxton, despite both candidates receiving A ratings from crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto.

Paxton has called for the passage of the CLARITY Act, arguing that clear regulations are necessary to maintain US competitiveness in digital assets. In a June statement, he said the country must become the “crypto capital of the world” and support innovation and growth.

Talarico has similarly called for clear regulations that protect consumers while allowing innovation. As a Texas state representative, he also voted in 2025 to establish a state strategic Bitcoin reserve, according to legislative records cited by Stand With Crypto.

In Wyoming, Republican Rep. Harriet Hageman faces Democrat James Byrd for the seat being vacated by Sen. Cynthia Lummis, a leading cryptocurrency advocate who is not seeking reelection.

Lummis, who chairs the Senate Banking Subcommittee on Digital Assets, sponsored the BITCOIN Act of 2025 to establish a US strategic Bitcoin reserve and has supported the CLARITY and GENIUS Acts.

Hageman has also backed cryptocurrency legislation, voting to pass the CLARITY and GENIUS Acts in 2025 and the FIT21 market structure bill in 2024. She also cosponsored legislation seeking to prohibit the Federal Reserve from issuing a central bank digital currency, according to Stand With Crypto. The advocacy group lists no documented position on cryptocurrency for Byrd.

Maine senator Susan Collins was one of four Republicans to oppose advancing the CLARITY Act in September, expressing concerns over its impact on bank deposits. She now faces Democrat Troy Jackson to defend her seat.

Unlike Collins, Jackson has expressed support for clear cryptocurrency legislation in the run-up to the election. According to Stand with Crypto, which rates Jackson as “very pro crypto”, he called to end the debanking of “lawful crypto businesses, and ensure Americans’ right to self-custody digital assets.”

Fairshake, backed by Coinbase, Ripple and Andreessen Horowitz, has led the spending through its affiliated super PACs, Defend American Jobs and Protect Progress. In early October, the group announced support for 32 incumbent House candidates, including 19 Republicans and 13 Democrats, all of whom voted in favor of the CLARITY Act in 2025.

Other crypto-aligned groups include the Digital Freedom Fund, backed by Tyler and Cameron Winklevoss, the twin co-founders of crypto exchange Gemini, and Fellowship PAC, which has supported candidates from both parties.

Weta FX and TAICCA Partner to Turn Taiwan Into an AI Screen Production Hub for Asia (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Weta FX, the New Zealand visual effects house behind “The Lord of the Rings” and “Avatar,” is teaming with the Taiwan Creative Content Agency (TAICCA) on a plan to turn Taiwan into a center for AI-driven screen production in Asia.

The partnership will be formally launched on Nov. 6 at Taiwan Creative Content Fest (TCCF) in Taipei. The launch takes place on the festival’s main stage right after the opening ceremony. The partnership will have three strands: joint investment, AI research and development, and training new talent. Related Stories Sega's 'Sonic the Hedgehog' to Launch Lingokids Games (EXCLUSIVE)

Specifics of the arrangement are being held back for the launch.

TAICCA chair Sue Wang will appear at the event alongside three Weta FX executives: CEO Daniel Seah, chief technology officer Kimball Thurston and VFX supervisor Sheldon Stopsack.

The pact pairs Taiwan’s AI and advanced-tech capabilities and its pool of creative talent with what Weta FX brings to the table – VFX know-how, a track record on international productions and a global network. The aim is to plug Taiwanese technologists and creatives into work across the worldwide screen business.

For Seah, the deal carries a personal angle. He grew up in Taiwan. The studio has also been building ties on the island, appearing at the Taoyuan International Film & Technology Forum in August.

The tie-up fits within TAICCA’s wider brief, which takes in the use of new technologies to make content. TTXC Innovations, a recently concluded market platform, is one case in point. It was built to help bring cultural content and tech to market by putting creators, tech developers, investors, buyers and distributors in the same room.

Taiwan’s Ministry of Culture set up TAICCA in 2019. The agency backs Taiwanese content from development through production, distribution and overseas marketing, across film, TV, animation, publishing, comics, music, gaming and the performing arts. Its toolkit includes investment, co-production schemes, industry programs and partnerships abroad.

Weta FX is based in Wellington, with outposts in Vancouver and Melbourne. It has won eight Oscars for visual effects, 17 Academy Science & Technology Awards, eight BAFTAs for special visual effects, 10 VES Awards in the photoreal feature category and five Emmys. Its latest work on the “Avatar” franchise was 2025’s “Avatar: Fire and Ash.” Titles in production at the studio include “The Lord of the Rings: The Hunt for Gollum,” Marvel Studios’ “Avengers: Doomsday” and “Children of Blood and Bone.”

Google offers weak response to accusations it would hold Search hostage if schools restrict AI tools

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Google LLC just faced a barrage of criticism after the company warned that if schools restricted their students from using its artificial intelligence tools, the same students wouldn’t be able to use Search.

The fallout came after the company announced a slew of new products for classrooms. Teachers and schools were told that the new AI tools would come with features that will allow for limitations or complete blocks of the features, but if teachers or schools should take that route, students would not be able to use Google Search. Some media described it as rather cunning behavior from the tech giant.

“After listening to schools, teachers, leading researchers, and policymakers, we are now updating our approach to controls for Gemini, Gemini Notebook, Gemini in Classroom, and Google Search,” Google said in a blog post. “Access to these products will be entirely off for K-12 student accounts until school administrators explicitly turn them on.”

A New York Times article followed the announcement and explained that where schools have banned the use of AI or if they limit AI in the classroom, the students would not have access to Search. With Google’s suit of products being a mainstay in classrooms all over the U.S., the backlash was imminent.

“Google is trying to use its monopoly power to force AI into our public schools,” New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani wrote on X. “No corporation should have the power to dictate how we teach our children. Our public schools exist to serve students, not to grow the profit margins of Big Tech.”

Sen. Bernie Sanders from Vermont was equally chastising, writing on X that, “Google is threatening schools,” and using what amounts to, “extortion.” Lina Khan, former chair of the U.S. Federal Trade Commission, was similarly unimpressed, writing that Google was trying to, “coerce schools into adopting AI”.

Google was quick to respond. In a post on X, the company said its new policy is about giving schools more control over how students use AI. Gemini chatbot, Gemini Notebook, the company explained, will be turned off by default, but “schools are in control.”

“We are also updating Search for schools to make it better for learning,” Google added. “Specifically, AI Mode and AI Overviews will offer step-by-step guided learning through problems, so that students don’t get quick answers to schoolwork.” The company explained that it was aware of the dangers of students embracing the “AI shortcut” and effectively not engaging their brains.

In what seemed like a sleight of hand or exercise in obfuscation, Google seemed to ignore the basis of the criticism regarding Search not being available when AI functions were turned off. Given the company has found itself under the gun many times in recent years for its sometimes-underhand business tactics, and in light of studies showing overuse of technology can have a harmful impact on children, the response seemed tepid at best.

Viettel Group and Vietnam's Government Cipher Committee Unveil Security Chip Embedding Vietnamese Cryptography in Silicon

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

HANOI, Vietnam, Nov. 3, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Vietnam's Government Cipher Committee and Viettel Group have unveiled VGC v1.0, a security chip integrating a domestically developed cryptographic algorithm directly into silicon, alongside the country's first domestically designed secure semiconductor platform.

The project addresses concerns over potential data exposure associated with reliance on imported chips. By embedding cryptographic functions directly into hardware, Vietnam's Government Cipher Committee and Viettel Group aim to strengthen Vietnam's control over the design and verification of technologies used to protect sensitive information.

VGC v1.0 contains 300 million transistors in a 10 Ã 10 mm package and is fabricated using a 28-nanometre CMOS process. In performance tests, it performed encryption and decryption approximately 15 times faster than a software-only solution carrying out the same tasks. The first batch passed technical tests and received certification for socio-economic applications in the first quarter of 2026. Technical safeguards were also applied to protect the design before fabrication, reducing risks of information leakage and reverse engineering.

The Government Cipher Committee's M2 unit established security requirements, developed the cryptographic algorithm and defined evaluation criteria, while Viettel Semiconductor Center designed and implemented the chip. Based on the open RISC-V instruction set architecture, the underlying platform enables Vietnamese engineers to modify processor designs and reuse established verification processes for future chip generations.

VGC v1.0 has already seen initial deployment in secure USB storage devices for Party, government and defence agencies. Planned applications include supporting Vietnam's national digital signature certification system, which has more than two million digital certificates in circulation. Other potential uses span financial data protection, connected cameras and sensors, and critical infrastructure systems in electricity, water, oil and gas, and telecommunications. Products intended to protect state secrets would require further testing and certification under applicable standards.

"Researching, designing and developing secure semiconductor chips is a formidable challenge, requiring years of accumulated expertise, technological capabilities and highly skilled talent. Yet we believe that starting later does not mean accepting dependence on others. With the right strategy, focused resources and sustained commitment, we can steadily close the gap and move towards mastering technologies of strategic importance to our country," said Lieutenant General Tao Duc Thang, Chairman and CEO of Viettel Group.

At the launch, the Vietnam's Government Cipher Committee and Viettel Group signed an agreement covering plans for volume production, multicore security chips, specialist workforce development and testing on real devices. Viettel is also building a semiconductor fabrication plant in Hoa Lac as part of broader efforts to develop domestic capabilities across chip design, manufacturing and packaging.

Beyond the initial chip, the initiative establishes a foundation for future generations of security hardware, supporting Vietnam's long-term efforts to reduce technological dependence and strengthen control over critical digital technologies.

Should You Buy Alphabet Stock After Anthropic Raised Its Google Commitment to $121 Billion?

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Claude maker just raised its minimum spending commitment to Google. Can it pay?

Anthropic, the artificial intelligence (AI) company behind Claude, updated its initial public offering (IPO) filing on Monday, Nov. 2, adding its third-quarter results. On Sept. 30, it had $95.6 billion in cash and cash equivalents and $36.3 billion in short-term investments -- around $132 billion combined, near its total at the end of June.

Against this, Anthropic has signed purchase commitments and leases worth about $895.4 billion, almost all for compute and infrastructure. The first filing, on Oct. 26, left that figure blank.

And one of the largest parts goes to Alphabet (GOOGL +0.37%)(GOOG +0.08%). Anthropic now says it committed to spend at least about $121.3 billion with Google between April 2026 and July 2033, no matter how much it uses. Last week's filing had that number at about $114.5 billion.

With Alphabet shares near $283 as I write, is this durable revenue for Google Cloud, or a bet on a customer that lost money from operations last quarter? I think it's mainly the former over the next couple of years.

Google Cloud's revenue backlog (contracted revenue not yet recognized) hit $560.8 billion on Sept. 30. Three months earlier, it stood at $513.9 billion. And a year before, Alphabet's total backlog, mainly Google Cloud, was just $157.7 billion, under an older count that left out contracts for a year or less. But growth has slowed lately, with around $47 billion added in the third quarter versus almost $52 billion in the second.

Anthropic's $121.3 billion minimum equals around 22% of that cloud backlog. Alphabet doesn't say how much of the number comes from Anthropic, and some of the minimum has probably been spent since April. But it's a big share tied to one customer.

Spread across a bit more than seven years, the minimum averages around $16.5 billion a year. Google Cloud's third-quarter revenue climbed 103% year over year to $30.7 billion, a rate of around $123 billion a year. This puts Anthropic's average minimum at about 13% of the cloud unit's current sales -- important, but not the whole business.

Anthropic also estimates it could spend another $125 billion to $150 billion on Google's custom AI chips, though some of that might go through third-party providers rather than Google.

For the next year, Anthropic looks able to pay. Around $69.8 billion of its commitments are due in the 12 months after Sept. 30. Its cash and investments cover it almost twice, not counting $14.8 billion of credit it hasn't tapped or any proceeds from its IPO.

The business is helping. Third-quarter revenue climbed around 47% from the second quarter to about $17.0 billion, more than 12 times its revenue for the year-ago quarter. Multiply that by four, and it's close to what comes due in the next 12 months.

Operating cash flow for the first nine months of 2026 was a positive $6.6 billion, versus negative $2.0 billion for the same period of 2025.

But growth cooled from the second quarter's 144% sequential jump. Anthropic had a third-quarter operating loss of $567 million, which included around $2.0 billion of non-cash charitable contributions. A year before, it lost $3.1 billion.

Still, compute and infrastructure costs rose back to 58% of revenue in the third quarter, against 48% in the second, but they were 148% of revenue a year ago.

Later on, the bills get far bigger. Around $447.5 billion of the commitments are expected before the end of 2030 -- about $105 billion a year, well over Anthropic's current revenue pace.

"We must grow our customer base and expand revenue rapidly in order to generate the cash flows necessary to satisfy these commitments," Anthropic wrote in the filing.

If Anthropic spends less than its minimum, it must pay Google the difference. Of course, that payment only helps Google if Anthropic has the cash. And Google has agreed to provide up to $20 billion of financing, in convertible notes or stock, subject to conditions, so part of what Anthropic pays Google might come from Google itself.

Alphabet doesn't need Anthropic to work out perfectly for its stock to make sense. Shares trade at around 19 times expected 2027 earnings, about 31% under their 52-week high. And Google Cloud earned $11.1 billion of operating income in the third quarter, around three times the $3.6 billion it made a year before.

Does the amended filing change my take on Alphabet? Not much. But I'd treat the next few years of the Google commitment as solid revenue and the later years as a bet on Anthropic's growth.

Overall, I think Alphabet stock is still worth a look at this price.

Daniel Sparks and his clients do not have positions in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Alphabet. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

AI cloud startup Applied Compute raises $350M after reaching key revenue milestone

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Applied Compute Inc., a fast-growing provider of artificial intelligence infrastructure, has raised $350 million in funding at a $3.25 billion valuation.

The startup announced the deal on Monday. It stated that prominent angel investor Elad Gil led the round with participation from Nvidia Corp., Shopify Ventures, HubSpot Ventures and over a dozen others.

Applied Compute operates a cloud platform that developers use to train large language models and perform inference. Additionally, it has a team of machine learning researchers that can lend customers a hand with their projects. The group takes on tasks such as creating quality evaluations for newly developed models.

The software stack that AI researchers use to train LLMs usually comprises two main components. One creates multiple copies of the model, assigns a different task to each one and turns the results into training data. The other component uses that training data to refine the LLM’s weights. The two components usually run synchronously, which means that each one has to wait for the other to complete its work before launching.

Applied Compute’s platform uses a more efficient training approach called asynchronous RL. It enables a training stack’s data generation and weight adjustment components to run at the same time, which speeds up LLM development. Additionally, the platform supports a second optimization method called self-distillation. It removes the need for teacher models, specialized neural networks that researchers use to supervise LLM training runs.

A built-in AI assistant automates repetitive aspects of customers’ work. It can, for example, summarize what new skills an LLM learned during a training run. Uncovering that information usually requires researchers to review lengthy reasoning logs.

Applied Computing’s platform also lends itself to running inference workloads. An autoscaling engine automatically adjusts the amount of hardware allocated to a model based on user traffic. A so-called online RL feature uses the prompts that the LLM receives to periodically fine-tune its weights, which keeps it aligned with customer requirements.

Applied Computing offers its training and inference features alongside a model router called Shift-1. It enables coding agents to send each user request to the LLM that can fulfill it in the most cost-efficient manner. Furthermore, it can split complex tasks among multiple LLMs.

Applied Computing disclosed in its funding announcement that its annualized recurring revenue has topped $100 million. The company, which launched less than 18 months ago, counts Microsoft Corp., DoorDash Inc. and other major tech firms as customers. It will use the newly raised capital to grow its market presence and buy more infrastructure.

“Training, inference, and routing are the first pieces of a much larger stack,” Applied Compute founders Rhythm Garg, Yash Patil and Linden Li wrote in a blog post. “We’re building the AI infrastructure every company will need to fully own their intelligence, from the models themselves to the systems that run, monitor, and secure them.”

AMD rides data center riches to &#8216;largest and most profitable&#8217; quarter in history

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

AMD thinks over 80 percent of its revenue will eventually come from its data center business alone.﻿

AMD thinks over 80 percent of its revenue will eventually come from its data center business alone.﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

AMD CEO Lisa Su said in the company’s quarterly earnings call on Tuesday that Q3 2026 was “was the largest and most profitable in our history.” AMD reported a record $13.4 billion in quarterly revenue, and it’s predicting it will hit another record high next quarter of $17 billion. The company’s data center business is driving that growth, with $8.5 billion in data center quarterly revenue for Q3 2026. Su said AMD’s expecting its revenue to “grow more than 80 percent in 2027,” adding that “data center will be the largest contributor.”

Eventually, most of AMD’s revenue might come from its data center business. In response to a question during Tuesday’s earnings call, CFO Jean Hu said, “Looking ahead of 2027, we actually think our data center business will be more than 80 percent of total company revenue.”

As of Q3 2026, roughly 63 percent of AMD’s net quarterly revenue was from its data center business. Over the last couple years, jumps in the company’s revenue have been closely mirroring its data center business — in Q3 2026 its overall revenue went up $1.9 billion, while its data center revenue went up $1.8 billion.

As AMD keeps smashing its data center revenue records, its gaming business dropped 40 percent compared to last year, dipping to $773 million. PCs, game consoles, and other tech have been hit with price hikes over recent months due to shortages of RAM and other components, driven by demand from AI data centers. The shortage might not end anytime soon — Intel said it’s not expecting the shortage to improve next year, and SK Hynix predicted it’ll last until 2030.

Su acknowledged the price hikes, saying, “Our view is, this is one of those times — when, you know, component prices go up, it affects overall system pricing. One would expect the consumer markets to be more sensitive to that, and so we have seen a bit of a slowdown in the sell-through.” Su added, “We are planning with the current environment for a down year for 2027.”

SURROUND Presents: Why Good Product Design Requires More Than a Good Idea

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A well-designed product can solve problems, but getting that product into the hands of the people who need it is another challenge entirely. Increasingly, designers are being asked to think beyond form and function to consider business models, manufacturing, marketing, and more.

In the latest episode of SURROUND Presents, METROPOLIS Editor-in-Chief Avi Rajagopal explores this evolving role with Souvik Paul, founder of medical device startup Aurie, and Sinclair Smith of the School of Visual Arts (SVA) MFA Products of Design program. Their conversation examines what it takes to move from identifying a problem to developing a viable solution—and why entrepreneurship is becoming an essential part of a designer’s education.

For Paul, the journey began on Wall Street, where he worked in finance before deciding to pursue a career in industrial design. Just two weeks before starting graduate school at SVA, his future sister-in-law sustained a spinal cord injury in a car accident. The experience fundamentally changed his approach to design, prompting him to dedicate his studies to understanding how products and services could improve the lives of people living with disabilities.

That work eventually led to Aurie, a medical device startup developing a reusable catheter system for people living with spinal cord injuries, spina bifida, and multiple sclerosis. Unlike conventional single-use catheters, Aurie’s catheter can be reused up to 100 times. The compact system automatically cleans, disinfects, and lubricates the catheter between uses. The approach reconsiders not only the physical product but also the economics of continued catheter use.

Yet designing the system was only one part of the challenge. After graduating, Paul worked as a design strategist at Johnson & Johnson, where he began to understand the difficulties of bringing new medical technologies to market. He eventually realized that if he wanted Aurie to exist, he would need to build the business behind it.

“If I believe that this product could—and should—exist in the world, I’m gonna have to do the legwork,” Paul recalled on SURROUND Presents. “I’m gonna have to take it across the finish line.”

That realization speaks to a broader shift in design education. At SVA, Smith has been rethinking how the MFA Products of Design program prepares students to develop ideas beyond the prototype stage. Drawing on his experience running the school’s Ground Floor Incubator, he has integrated business modeling and entrepreneurship more directly into the curriculum, allowing students to develop the commercial frameworks for their thesis projects alongside the products themselves.

Paul’s experience illustrates this broader approach. For him, the skills developed through design education continue to inform his work, whether he’s collaborating with other designers, communicating with investors, or navigating the regulatory requirements of medical device development.

The conversation ultimately highlights how designers increasingly need to understand the larger systems that determine how—and whether—their ideas can make a difference.

Listen to the full episode of SURROUND Presents, presented by SVA, to hear Avi Rajagopal’s conversation with Souvik Paul and Sinclair Smith.

Paramount+ Drama Slate: Series Moving To HBO, Sarah Aubrey & Francesca Orsi Sharing Oversight Among Options Explored Post-Merger – The Dish

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Almost a month into Paramount’s acquisition of Warner Bros Discovery into Skydance, pieces are still coming together, and there is probably no area with more pieces to sort out than Skydance’s Direct to Consumer. With the content operations of Paramount+ and HBO/HBO Max combined under Casey Bloys, now Co-Chair and Chief Content Officer, Skydance DTC, the former HBO & HBO Content CEO saw his portfolio more than double overnight.

The expansion is passed on to his senior team which he brought over from HBO/HBO Max, led by Francesca Orsi, Amy Gravitt and Sarah Aubrey, with Paramount+’s EVP and Head of Originals Jane Wiseman leaving as part of the restructuring announced October 23. Watch on Deadline

Organizing the combined slate has been a priority for the new regime as it is reviewing Paramount+’s development and production pipeline. The process is in early stages and sources stress that no decisions have been made, but various ideas are being discussed, including possibly relocating upcoming Paramount+ drama series to HBO, I hear.

Logical targets for a move to HBO are legal thriller Discretion, starring Big Little Lies‘ Nicole Kidman and Elle Fanning, which has frequently been described as HBO-like; 9/11 first responders limited series The Best of Us, which is headlined by Emmy-winning Succession star Jeremy Strong; as well as a couple of projects awaiting official green light.

There have been conversations, which I hear have reached the highest levels, but no migrations, with all series earmarked for Paramount+ remaining there for the time being. Talks are continuing.

Even if the two streamers’ slates remain status quo — they are bound to merge eventually as Skydance co-CEO David Ellison has revealed plans for HBO Max and Paramount+ to become one platform in the next couple of years — there could be an HBO stamp on some upcoming Paramount+ dramas via potential involvement by Orsi.

While Paramount+ has been staying largely out of the original comedy series game, BET+’s The Varnell Hill Show being a rare exception, its original drama slate is extensive, led by the flagship Taylor Sheridan universe. (The streamer also has next-day comedies and drama procedurals coming from CBS.)

In the new structure, HBO Max’s Head of Originals Aubrey, now Head of Paramount+, Max Originals and International Programming, added oversight of all Paramount+ programming, with Wiseman’s team reporting to her.

As part of the same realignment, Orsi was promoted from EVP to President, HBO Drama Series and Films in an expanded new role that involves her working with Bloys on determining which drama projects are best suited for HBO.

I hear that may involve her taking oversight of Paramount+ original dramas that carry HBO DNA, whether they relocate to HBO or not.

Aubrey and Orsi are currently reviewing the Paramount+ drama slate together, and I hear there is a possibility for Orsi to work on shows that stay on Paramount+ whenever it makes sense. Those are expected to be in the prestige drama lane that HBO is known for.

7 Greatest Netflix Miniseries That Are Worth Watching Over and Over

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Content Warning: This list contains a brief mention of sexual assault.There's no question that Netflix has some fantastic miniseries, from Bodyguard to Bodies. But there are two types of great ones: ones you want to watch, let them sit with you, talk to others about them, and move on to the next. Others, however, call for a second watch. It might be to catch things you missed or view the story from a different perspective now that you know how it ends.

Whatever the reason, a handful of Netflix miniseries fall into the latter category, though for differing reasons. Interestingly, five of the six are powerful, heavy stories. But that's why watching them when you're in different frames of mind could cause you to see the stories in a different light.

There are a lot of "WTF" moments in Midnight Mass, especially if you go in without knowing what it's about. I watched after already knowing the big twist, but I can imagine that those who were thrown for a loop would want to go back and pick up on missed clues. The Gothic supernatural horror miniseries stars Zach Gilford as Riley Flynn, a young man struggling to acclimate to life in his small town after spending time in prison. Desperate to maintain his sobriety and find purpose, he is approached by the town's mysterious new priest, Father Paul Hill (Hamish Linklater).

The story in arguably the best Mike Flanagan TV show goes from small-town mundane life to scary religious story. Father Paul, it seems, is not your average man. And the reasons he gave for taking over for the town's long-time priest, Monsignor Pruitt, aren't exactly as he explained. The story heavily explores religious themes, including the idea of fanatical religion. It's a serious topic, and you may see things differently on a re-watch. Outside of that, Midnight Mass is thrilling with bumps in the night, creepy moments, and fantastically nuanced and emotional performances.

Unorthodox is an emotional tale of hope, and with just four episodes, it's an easy binge a second or third time around. Esty Shapiro (Shira Haas) is a young Jewish woman living in a strict Orthodox community in Brooklyn. She yearns for more, and though she is newly married to a man her family picked for her, and learns she is pregnant, she realizes there's no time like the present to escape and explore a secular lifestyle. So, she runs.

The story in this German drama, told in a mix of English, Yiddish, and German, follows both Esty's journey to self-discovery and the panic of her family members, including her husband Yaakov (Amit Rahav) to find her and bring her home, at all costs. It's a relatable story for anyone who has ever felt oppressed by their strict upbringing, whether by religious beliefs or something else. Knowing it's inspired by the true story of Deborah Feldman makes Unorthodox all the more impactful. For something uplifting that depicts a courageous woman at the center of the plot, Unorthodox will make you feel the same satisfying sense of relief every single time you watch.

Adolescence should come with a warning, because if you go into it like I did, just hearing from someone that you should watch without much context as to why, your jaw will be on the floor. That will happen again and again, no matter how often you watch the nearly perfect miniseries. But each time, you'll get something more out of it. The story in the psychological crime drama is that 13-year-old Jamie Miller (Owen Cooper) is arrested for the murder of a schoolmate. His parents are confused and horrified as they follow their son to the precinct for questioning. When they hear a story and see supposed evidence, it rocks them to their core.

The story continues to explore Jamie's journey as he sits with forensic psychologist Briony Ariston (Erin Doherty) and his parents, Eddie (Stephen Graham) and Manda (Christine Tremarco), do their best to continue on while dealing with the aftermath of this heinous crime. The episodes are hard to watch, every one of them filmed in a single take without anything but dialogue to drive them. Once you get over the initial shock, you'll want to listen carefully to the words, the guilt, the tone, in every scene to truly understand the damage that social media can have on young minds, and the impact on their loved ones. It's not a show that's just so good, it's worth watching again and again. It's one you'll want to watch again with your own teens, even the whole family, and use it as a point of discussion about difficult topics. Adolescence isn't about guilt or innocence, it's an examination of cyberbullying, the internet, and the manosphere in a way that seems almost too raw and real to be fiction.

Another show with high shock value, Baby Reindeer takes you on a journey with Donny Dunn (Richard Gadd) in a story purportedly based on Gadd's own experiences. There are two major things he's dealing with. A woman becomes obsessed with him after he's flirty at the bar where he works, and she relentlessly stalks him. He's also a struggling comedian looking to make it big and is taken advantage of by a TV writer. All the while, he's struggling with his own sexuality.

Once you get through the seven episodes, you may want to re-watch to dive into each separate story again. You'll want to try and view things through Donny's lens and those of other characters, like his parents, his transgender girlfriend Teri (Nava Mau), and even Martha (Jessica Gunning). Seeing all the moments that led to Donny's emotional breakdown hit harder the second time around as well. The third time? You'll still be grabbing for tissues.

Four Netflix characters, four completely different ways of showing up for somebody. This is the one who fits how you actually do friendship, and what having them in your life would be like.

You do not do friendship at half volume, and neither does he. What you want is somebody who says yes to the plan before you have finished describing the plan.

You are the friend who makes things happen, and so is she. Together you would be mildly exhausting to everyone around you and completely delighted with yourselves.

You have no patience for pleasant lies, and she has no ability to tell them. What looks like coldness from the outside is the most reliable friendship on this list.

You look after whoever is having the worst day. So does he. It is the least strategic way to move through the world and the best possible quality in a friend.

A story of hope, perseverance, and rising above, you might be able to watch The Queen's Gambit now with a clearer lens versus the one masked by COVID-19 stay-at-home orders when it first came out. And you will thoroughly enjoy the story through all seven episodes of the coming-of-age period drama. Set in the 1950s and based on the Walter Tevis novel, Beth Harmon (Anya Taylor-Joy) is a young orphan who discovers that she's a chess prodigy.

The show that makes for a perfect weekend binge follows Beth's rise up the ranks in the chess world while simultaneously dealing with her addiction to both drugs and alcohol. While Beth is proving herself a worthy player against all these men, she's also learning about her own worth as a person. The drugs that help her visualize the moves are a metaphor for the insecurities that hold Beth back and make her doubt her own abilities. It's a beautiful story of redemption and a young woman coming into her own, overcoming obstacles and fighting for her place.

In 1989, five Black and Latino young men were charged with the rape and assault of a young woman in Central Park. But years later, the convictions were all overturned when another man confessed to the crime. If you know the story of the Central Park Five, you may have been interested in watching this crime drama. If you didn't, the four episodes of When They See Us will walk you through everything. As a Netflix miniseries that will hook you in the first five minutes, you won't be able to resist binging your way through the entire thing. It's a gripping retelling of a story that rocked a city and country and continues to be talked about and sadly relevant to this day.

As one of the most talked about cases of wrongful conviction, The Central Park Five case, and in relation to this show, is a powerful commentary on racism and racial profiling. It doesn't get any easier to watch a second or third time around, especially knowing that When They See Us is inspired by real life events and real people deeply hurt by accusations at such tender, young ages. The powerful performances from the lead actors and the poignant messaging are worth exploring again and again, opening eyes to a terrible injustice that continues to repeat.

Looking for something lighter? Classic rom-coms are one of the most rewatchable genres, and while One Day, a romantic drama, hasn't been around long enough to be considered a classic, it may very well become one a decade or two from now. The lovely story in the romantic drama follows Emma Morley (Ambika Mod) and Dexter Mayhew (Leo Woodall) through 14 years of ups and downs in their relationship.

Based on the David Nicholls novel as well as the 2011 film adaptation, One Day is 14 episodes long, but it's the perfect binge when you're feeling romantic, even lonely. It will satiate your desire for an emotional, sweet, and well-delivered romantic drama that spans a decade and-a-half of time. It takes you on a long yet digestible journey through this likable couple's time together.

Binance stopped 57 North Korean cyber threats but fell for a fake job interview

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The exchange's defenses thwarted dozens of suspected attacks before hackers used a staged recruitment process to infiltrate its wallet systems.

Binance said it thwarted 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyber threats in 2026, yet a fake interview compromised one of its engineers.

The disclosure, in the exchange's Nov. 3 security report, reveals how attackers used a recruitment scam involving staged interviews, fabricated identities, and malicious software to access systems involved in the $6.34 billion cryptocurrency theft on Oct. 29.

“Throughout 2026, Binance's security team detected and prevented 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyber threat cases. These attempts reflect the persistent threat posed by state-sponsored actors targeting the cryptocurrency industry. The techniques include fraudulent job interviews, malicious software disguised as popular developer tools, compromised software packages and browser extensions, fake video conferencing applications, and social engineering campaigns designed to steal credentials or deploy remote access malware. We intercepted many of them, but the cost of failing to stop even one was severe—and this should never have happened.”

The operation succeeded despite Binance's security team identifying and preventing dozens of suspected North Korean intrusion attempts during the year, including campaigns using fraudulent job interviews and compromised developer tools.

The FBI attributed the theft to North Korea's TraderTraitor operation on Oct. 31, describing an ongoing effort to steal cryptocurrency and launder the proceeds through decentralized finance platforms.

Binance's findings expose a troubling gap between recognizing a persistent threat and preventing its execution.

The campaign's earliest identified stage occurred in June, when a Binance wallet infrastructure engineer downloaded a tampered Homebrew installer while attempting to install a popular AI coding tool.

Binance's security team detected the suspicious software, replaced the device, and rotated related credentials. However, the incident exposed information about the engineer's responsibilities and colleagues.

In September, someone impersonating a project director at a fictitious company approached another software engineer through a legitimate recruitment platform.

An individual posing as a human resources representative conducted a live video interview and shared a social media profile containing personal photographs and videos to establish credibility.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

A second interview followed with someone claiming to be the company's chief executive.

Near the end of that conversation, the engineer was instructed to download a coding assignment onto his work computer. The repository contained malware that executed during setup, replacing a legitimate developer-tool component and establishing communication with an attacker-controlled server.

Binance said the previously unknown malware evaded its endpoint security software and concealed traces of the intrusion.

By Oct. 20, the attackers had used the compromised computer and an internal AI tool to gain control over two additional engineers, including the employee targeted in June.

They subsequently obtained a container deployment permission token. Nine days later, the attackers used stolen credentials to deploy a malicious container and reach Binance's wallet-signing infrastructure.

The exchange said the attackers initiated unauthorized transfers outside its normal withdrawal process, draining assets across several blockchain networks.

The tactics closely resemble a North Korean cyber operation that security researchers and US authorities have tracked for years.

In September 2024, the FBI warned that North Korean hackers were using highly personalized employment opportunities, elaborate fictional identities, and prolonged conversations to infiltrate cryptocurrency businesses.

The agency noted that even technically sophisticated employees could be deceived by campaigns tailored to their professional backgrounds.

That playbook remained active throughout 2026.

In June, Kudelski Security researchers documented North Korea's Contagious Interview operation, in which hackers impersonated recruiters on LinkedIn, WhatsApp and Discord before persuading developers to execute malicious code.

By September, Atlassian researchers had identified fake technical assessments hosted on established development platforms, where malicious instructions were hidden within otherwise legitimate-looking software projects.

The similarities raise questions about why Binance's existing threat detection measures failed to identify the successful recruitment campaign.

The exchange has not disclosed how many of the 57 prevented cases involved fake interviews or whether any were directly connected to the group behind the October breach.

Following the attack, Binance said it replaced compromised employee devices, revoked exposed credentials, and rebuilt affected wallet infrastructure. It also strengthened developer-environment protections and expanded AI-based behavioral monitoring.

All the news about The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahead of the release of Rockstar Games’ Grand Theft Auto VI, Nintendo is launching its own fall juggernaut: a Switch 2 remake of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time.

The remake, which is releasing 28 years after the launch of the original version on Nintendo 64, has a much more realistic visual style and some modern improvements like a jump button, a way to sprint, full voice acting, and a “Threads of Time” feature that helps you keep track of your progress. But while the game has a new coat of paint, it appears many elements will still remain faithful to the classic N64 release. Nintendo has also launched a special version of the Switch 2 and Switch 2 Pro Controller with Triforce imagery to celebrate the game.

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake is scheduled to release on November 5th. You can follow along with all of our coverage of the game right here.

For the Ocarina of Time remake, Nintendo developers said in a new interview that they wanted to “preserve the experience of spamming the Roll Attack to forward roll your way across Hyrule Field.” I have very fond memories of rolling around the world in the original.

In the remake, you can still spam Roll Attack, but you can also dash.

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time launches later this week, and in the lead-up Nintendo has put out the first half of a lengthy developer Q&A, covering everything from what changed gameplay-wise, to the new visual style, which was inspired by puppet shows. As art director Aya Shida explains:

Neither puppet shows nor practical effects films reproduce reality exactly as it is. Even so, both create a convincing sense that the characters truly exist within their respective fantasy worlds. We also felt that there was some overlap between these forms of expression and the distinctive nostalgia evoked by the original game.

The long-awaited remake of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time for Nintendo Switch 2 launches next Thursday, November 5th. In other words, you have a week remaining to secure your physical edition with a $10 discount at Amazon and Walmart. Once the game launches, the physical version will almost certainly go from $59.99 to $69.99 while the digital version will stay at $59.99.

Nintendo had the unenviable task of reimagining its Nintendo 64-era masterpiece for the Switch 2, and based on our hands-on time with some sections of the game, it’s a lot of fun to play. The graphics and art style are fantastic, and the controls have been modernized to be simpler to pick up for those who are already experienced with third-person action games, including other modern Zelda titles.

I’ve really warmed up to the game’s art style, especially in this new trailer. And after reading my colleague Cameron Faulkner’s hands-on preview, I’m looking forward to returning to Hyrule even more. The game launches on Switch 2 on November 5th.

With its upcoming remake for the Switch 2, Nintendo might have nailed the best thing about The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time: its timelessness. The 1998 3D action adventure game for the Nintendo 64 that pioneered so much — 3D graphics, movement, exploration, openness — has been reengineered with changes that make it feel more modern, and surprisingly terrifying at times. Based on my early hands-on time with the game, it’s shaping up to be the best-looking, best-playing version of the game that addresses the most frustrating aspects of playing the original.

More than just sanding off its roughest edges, Nintendo also fitted Ocarina of Time with modern trappings to make it more welcoming to those who were introduced to Zelda through more modern releases, like Breath of the Wild. The remake has a new progression marker, called Threads of Time, that’s viewable when you pause. It also has Zelda Notes integration within the Nintendo Switch app to let you view game maps and other info on your phone. Oh, it also has a dedicated jump button.

It was briefly shown during Nintendo’s The Legend of Zelda 40th Anniversary Direct today, but Hori’s website now has a listing for its $49.99 electronic ocarina. You can play your own songs by pushing buttons, play one of 12 pre-programmed songs from the games, use it with the Ocarina of Time remake’s sound recognition feature, or connect headphones for a private jam session.

Link’s next adventure now has a release date. During a Legend of Zelda stream for the franchise’s 40th anniversary, Nintendo announced that the upcoming Ocarina of Time remake for the Switch 2 is launching on November 5th. The release date makes Ocarina of Time one of just a few big-budget titles launching in the same month as Grand Theft Auto VI.

Nintendo first teased the remake in June, though it didn’t reveal much aside from a brief trailer. Today’s event provided a clearer glimpse at how the company is approaching remaking one of its most influential games and the first 3D Zelda adventure. The updated version of Ocarina of Time will be the first Zelda release that’s exclusive to the Switch 2, and longtime Zelda producer Eiji Aonuma described the remake as “a faithful interpretation using modern technologies.”

Nintendo announced a remake of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time during its Nintendo Direct presentation on Tuesday. It will launch sometime in 2026 on Nintendo Switch 2. More details will be announced “in the future.”

This new title is the first major release in the franchise since 2024’s Echoes of Wisdom, which starred Princess Zelda herself. Echoes followed 2023’s Tears of the Kingdom, a direct sequel to Breath of the Wild — both of which got upgraded Switch 2 versions alongside the launch of the new console last year. In November, Nintendo also launched the hack-and-slash title Hyrule Warriors: Age of Imprisonment.

How Jon Ossoff Won Re-Election and Became the Midterms’ Main Character in the Process

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Georgia Democrat’s “Epstein class” stump speech turned a safe-seat race into a national obsession and must-watch politics, and even brought some 2028 chatter.

Democrat Jon Ossoff is about to win re-election in the Senate, with CNN projecting he will take down his opponent, the MAGA-friendly U.S. Rep. Mike Collins, by a wide margin.

While the more compelling Senate contest for many political observers have been the toss-ups in Maine, Michigan and Texas, the seemingly safe Georgia race became a fascination from inside the Beltway to the mansions of Brentwood as the midterms played out. This race had a little something for everyone.

Until recently, Ossoff was known for his 2020 runoff defeat of incumbent Republican David Perdue — a true nail-biter that grabbed global attention because his win, which was announced amid the January 6 Capitol Hill riots, meant Democrats would enter Joe Biden’s term with control of the Senate. Ossoff then laid low as a young Senate moderate who brokered some ho-hum bipartisan deals on drug prices until, in February, after an exhausting year of MAGA 2.0, the 39-year-old senator delivered a stump speech at the Georgia International Convention Center in which he characterized Trump as a “failed lame duck” who is deeply unpopular in Georgia and dubbed his administration the “Epstein class.” The speech spread widely online and brought in a wave of donations and, soon, attention from Hollywood’s elite. Now he’s one of the hottest Democrats in the country, and his declared lack of presidential ambitions hasn’t stopped chatter around a potential Ossoff 2028 bid. Related Stories Midterms 2026 Are the Midterms About to Be a Blowout Ratings Night for Cable News? Movies Jennifer Lawrence Cites 'Hunger Games' While Encouraging Fans to Vote: "Don't Forget Who Burns With Us"

How he got here is a story worth its own script treatment.

Ossoff was unopposed in the Democratic primary and spent the time sharpening his attacks on the Trump administration, hammering the president on inflation, foreign relations in the Middle East, immigration enforcement and the FBI raid that seized 2020 election documents in Fulton County. In the closing days of his reelection campaign, he questioned the objectives of the U.S. war in Iran and the administration’s official claims of military success. He cited record-high diesel prices and criticized tariffs and health care cuts. His pitch was summed up Monday at a speech in Decatur.

“We need change,” Ossoff told the crowd gathered for a final door-knocking push. “They have lied us into yet another war in the Middle East. They slashed Medicaid and doubled health insurance premiums to cut taxes for the rich. These tariffs have sent grocery prices soaring. They’re crushing Georgia’s small businesses and Georgia farms, but these next 26 hours are the time remaining for us to wield the power and the rights invested in us as citizens by our Constitution to make a difference.”

That criticism is decaf compared with what he saved for Trump himself. The first Millennial senator laid into the president in ways many would not have, alleging he preferred traveling with aide Natalie Harp and building an $800 million White House ballroom over fulfilling his presidential duties. He went on to accuse him of sleeping through meetings, golfing and trading stocks. Asked about Ossoff by the press, Trump offered a jab at his appearance, calling him a “Pee-wee Herman lookalike” and later mocking his $900 makeup session. The spat began in the context of Trump’s approval rating dipping below the 40 percent threshold and Ossoff’s polls moving up and to the right.

As the internet’s eyes turned to Ossoff over these campaign moments, Hollywood’s naturally followed, and the incumbent senator was enjoying the spoils of fundraising events held by television heavyweights Shonda Rhimes and Chuck Lorre, with maxed-out donations from Larry David, Sally Field and David Geffen, among other A-listers. The right has cried foul over these Hollywood connections in ways that seem to have fallen on deaf ears among Georgians concerned with kitchen-table issues and not whom the biz’s boomers are backing. Ossoff’s presidential ambitions are naturally questioned in this context, and as one politico reportedly told Page Six, if he bests his GOP rival today by 6 points, he’ll be all but shoved into a race against other potential 2028 Democratic favorites, California Gov. Gavin Newsom and former Vice President Kamala Harris.

The polls ahead of the vote had shown Ossoff with a commanding lead well over that 6-point mark; in fact, the final average had him +10 over Collins, whose backstory is an entirely different tale that involved a split in Georgia’s GOP. It began with a last-minute endorsement from Trump ahead of the primary runoff, which Collins earned over loyalty to the MAGA movement; i.e., he echoes, to this day, Trump’s big lie about his election defeat to Joe Biden in 2020. Collins’ runoff rival, Derek Dooley, has refuted these claims, which makes sense because he’s a childhood pal of Georgia Gov. Brian Kemp; the GOP initially wanted Kemp, who is exiting the governor’s office, to run against Ossoff, despite his clashes with Trump over election fraud. Dooley stood in as a surrogate but lost the runoff to the congressman and Trump loyalist.

With Collins out of the picture, Ossoff is now firmly in main-character position. Given that magnetic stump speech and the run he’s had since February, all eyes will on the senior senator from Georgia in the coming days, and certainly not for the last time.

iPad mini OLED vs. iPad mini: What's new?

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Apple's new iPad mini gets a bunch of upgrades, but they come at a cost.

Apple doesn't update its iPad mini every year, which is why each new generation usually gets a huge performance boost. The new iPad mini is powered by the 5-core, 2nm A20 Pro SoC, which Apple says is up to 2.7 times faster in graphics performance and up to 4.5 times faster in AI workloads than the A17 Pro in its predecessor. It comes in the same three storage configurations as last time, and the 512GB model features an extra CPU core and a dual 16-core Neural Engine.

While both models support Apple Intelligence and Siri AI, the A20 Pro should be more suited to demanding on-device AI tasks. The outgoing iPad mini offered 8GB of RAM, and while Apple doesn't officially list how much memory its phones and tablets have, the new iPad mini likely has 12GB of RAM. Battery life claims haven't changed, but the new iPad mini charges faster (capable of hitting 50 percent in 30 minutes) with a compatible adapter.

Unfortunately, color options have been reduced to just Midnight and Starlight. The price has also increased: the new iPad mini costs $200 more, starting at $699 for the 128GB model. If spec'd with cellular connectivity and a nano-texture glass finish, the 512GB iPad mini will set you back a whopping $1,249 — that's iPad Pro money. We'll let you decide if it's worth it; for now, let's look at the changes Apple has made to its compact tablet offering this year.

The big iPad mini upgrade this time is the OLED panel. That makes it the only other iPad in Apple's lineup with an OLED display aside from the iPad Pro. It's roughly the same resolution as last time, but can get much brighter at up to 1,200 nits of peak HDR brightness. OLED also brings other benefits: the new iPad mini is the same height and nearly the same width, but is thinner at 5.4mm and lighter at 255 grams — likely thanks to the thinner OLED panel. In comparison, the A17 Pro iPad mini maxes out at 500 nits of SDR brightness, weighs 293 grams and is almost an entire millimeter thicker at 6.3mm.

The new iPad mini also features a slightly larger 8.5-inch display, owing to the bezels that are now 24 percent thinner. The only major letdown is the lack of ProMotion. Plenty of users on X have understandably expressed disappointment about Apple sticking with a 60Hz display on a $700 product in 2026. However, in an interview with Christopher Lawley, Apple's vice president of Hardware Engineering, Dave Pakula, said that the "display experience is about more than just a single spec" and that OLED's faster pixel response time "makes everything really smooth."

If you're coming from an older iPad mini, the display on the new one will be a huge upgrade, especially for creative workflows involving HDR content. Apple Pencil Pro support is still present, too, making the new iPad mini a compact, color-accurate canvas for artists and designers.

Despite its nearly identical design, the new iPad mini is Apple's first water-resistant iPad. One of the ways it has achieved an IP67 rating is by replacing the speaker grilles with its new Acoustic Display system. This involves tweeters placed between the glass and aluminum body, with woofers firing through the display. The result, apparently, is punchier bass, clearer treble and more volume. It's similar to the Huawei P30 Pro's acoustic display, though that particular setup was largely designed to replace the earpiece speaker for phone calls rather than deliver a full stereo speaker experience like the iPad mini.

Connectivity upgrades are also present, including Wi-Fi 7, Bluetooth 6 and Apple's new C2 cellular modem system, which it claims should deliver up to 50 percent faster cellular data speeds.  Authentication is still handled by the Touch ID-enabled power button, and while the iPad mini's optics seem largely unchanged, the front-facing camera is now positioned along the tablet's longer edge, making it better suited for video calls in landscape orientation.

The new iPad mini runs iPadOS 27 and supports Apple's most powerful on-device AI model with improved dictation and the option to adjust Siri's pace and expressiveness. While only the 512GB variant gets the extra CPU and Neural Engine cores, the 5-core A20 Pro SoC in the lower-end storage variants still delivers a substantial performance boost over the A17 Pro. With so many good AAA titles like Control and Death Stranding now available natively on the App Store, the new iPad mini is shaping up to be a portable powerhouse for gamers.

NYSE Adds Four Transfer Agents to Planned Tokenized Securities Venue

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: The New York Stock Exchange said on November 2 that Broadridge, Computershare, Equiniti and Superstate had joined the network supporting its proposed tokenized securities venue. There is a major distinction between an issuer-sponsored tokenized security and a synthetic token that tracks a stock price without representing an actual company share.

Broadridge separately described plans to provide on-chain governance services, underlining how much of a securities market happens after a trade. Corporate records have to survive splits, dividends, shareholder meetings and ownership disputes. If tokenization ignores those workflows, its speed gains may produce new headaches instead of an easier market.

The New York Stock Exchange said on November 2 that Broadridge, Computershare, Equiniti and Superstate had joined the network supporting its proposed tokenized securities venue. They add to previously announced relationships with Securitize and tZERO. The planned platform is not being presented as a fully operating public marketplace today; a launch depends on further steps, including regulatory approval. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Transfer agents do the often-invisible work of maintaining official shareholder records and supporting issuer functions. Their participation matters because a token representing a security needs clear ownership, corporate-action handling and investor rights as well as a way to change hands. Putting a token on a ledger does not automatically deliver proxy voting, dividends, reconciliations or legal recognition.

The news shifts tokenization from a discussion of trading speed toward institutional plumbing. The NYSE says the partners collectively have extensive issuer relationships, which could simplify future adoption if product designs are approved. It also suggests that incumbent infrastructure firms are not being bypassed; they are being brought into a possible new recordkeeping and settlement model.

A tokenized instrument must connect technical records with enforceable financial rights. Trading software can move a token quickly, but an issuer, custodian, transfer agent or settlement bank may still have to recognise the result. This is why tokenization announcements need to explain which part of the lifecycle is changing, not merely advertise that a blockchain is involved.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: DTCC’s tokenized securities infrastructure rollout and tokenized fund redemptions tested over a weekend. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

There is a major distinction between an issuer-sponsored tokenized security and a synthetic token that tracks a stock price without representing an actual company share. The NYSE announcement concerns a planned structure involving regulated participants. It does not establish that a retail customer can buy every listed stock as a 24-hour token immediately or that shareholder rights will be identical under every arrangement.

Real settlement infrastructure is judged by whether it works on bad days, not just during presentations. Reconciliation, asset eligibility, funding, corporate actions and recovery from errors remain central. Continuous technical operation can improve coordination, yet it does not create market liquidity or remove the obligations of regulated participants.

Watch for rule filings, regulatory decisions, supported asset classes, custody standards and the precise rights attached to tokenized shares. Operational details about voting and corporate actions will be as important as the underlying ledger. Until those details emerge, the partnerships demonstrate preparation and potential reach, not completed migration of Wall Street equity trading onto blockchains.

Jon Ossoff wins reelection in Georgia, easily defeating Republican Mike Collins

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sen. Jon Ossoff, D-Ga., speaks during a campaign event on Oct. 31, 2026, in Atlanta. Ryan Murphy/AP hide caption

Midterms coverage: Live results | What to know | Stories around the countryStay connected: NPR Politics Podcast | NPR Politics newsletter | NPR app

Jon Ossoff, the only incumbent Senate Democrat defending a seat in a state President Trump carried in 2024, has won the U.S. Senate race in Georgia.

Ossoff defeated Republican Rep. Mike Collins, according to a race call by The Associated Press.

A year ago, Democrats and Republicans envisioned the race in Georgia as one of the cycle's marquee Senate showdowns.

But in Collins, the GOP in Georgia nominated a candidate whose personal baggage alienated independents and some Republican-leaning voters.

In Ossoff, Democrats were buoyed by a candidate who won respect at home even from some Republican officials for the strength of his constituent services. And outside Georgia, Ossoff electrified Democrats searching for fresh faces to lead the party out of the political wilderness.

On paper, the race should have been closer. In 2021, Democrats flipped both of Georgia's Senate seats blue for the first time in a generation. In the 2022 midterms, Democratic Sen. Raphael Warnock barely held onto his seat in a runoff, even against a deeply flawed opponent.

Otherwise, Republicans have continued to win nearly every other statewide race in Georgia.

In the Republican primary this spring, Trump endorsed Collins over former football coach Derek Dooley, a first-time candidate backed by Georgia's popular Republican Gov. Brian Kemp.

Collins, the owner of a family trucking company, struggled to dispense with scrutiny over his ties to family and former staffers who have espoused white nationalist and antisemitic views. Collins also continued to embrace Trump's false claims about widespread election fraud in 2020.

Rep. Mike Collins, R-Ga., at a campaign rally on Oct. 24, 2026, in Perry, Ga. Mark Schiefelbein/AP hide caption

Ossoff, a former congressional aide and documentary filmmaker, first appeared on the national stage when he waged a closer-than-expected attempt to flip a suburban House race in a special election in 2017. After Georgia sent Ossoff to the Senate in a 2021 runoff, he gained attention for his methodical grilling of Trump appointees during hearings on the Hill.

As clips from Ossoff excoriating alleged graft in Trump's orbit in rally speeches ricocheted around the internet over the course of the campaign, many Democrats began to mention him as a possible presidential candidate. Ossoff has said he is not running for the White House in 2028.

Google responds to political backlash over removing search from schools that ban AI

📅 2026-11-04  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Google is playing defense on Tuesday after a political backlash to its new policy surrounding use of artificial intelligence in schools.

In a blog post titled "Designing responsible AI for learning," Google said it's bringing AI into its Classroom offering with a product called Guided Learning. The New York Times published a story saying that the company will turn off search for schools that reject AI entirely.

Following the Times' story, prominent left-leaning politicians, including Sen. Bernie Sanders from Vermont and New York Mayor Zohran Mamdani voiced their outrage on social media.

"Google is trying to use its monopoly power to force AI into our public schools," Mamdani wrote on X. Sanders wrote that "Google is threatening schools," referring to the policy as "extortion."

Google responded later in the day. In a post on X, the company said its new policy was meant to give schools more choice over how students interact with AI. Google said its Gemini chatbot, Gemini Notebook and search are turned off by default, and that "schools are in control," with the ability to turn them as they see fit.

"We are also updating Search for schools to make it better for learning," Google wrote. "Specifically, AI Mode and AI Overviews will offer step-by-step guided learning through problems, so that students don't get quick answers to schoolwork."

The company's post didn't explicitly respond to the claim that access to search requires use of AI. However, Chris Phillips, vice president for education at Google, told the Times that "Search and AI are deeply integrated."

A Google spokesperson referred CNBC to the X post when asked for comment.

Google's broader practices have come under fire in recent years, with the company losing antitrust cases over its search and advertising businesses. Former Federal Trade Commission Chair Lina Khan aggressively pursued Google and other tech giants, including Meta and Amazon.

Khan said in a post on X on Tuesday that Google's new AI for learning policy highlights "the danger of allowing an unregulated monopolist to control an essential service."

It's the latest controversy related to the impact of technology on children and the role leading internet companies are playing in delivering information.

In August, Meta reached a landmark $16.7 billion settlement in its child safety trial after a number of state attorneys general accused the company of misrepresenting the extent of child mental health harms caused by apps like Facebook and Instagram.

The same month, OpenAI launched ChatGPT for Teens after state attorneys general launched an investigation into the company's safety practices, with Florida Attorney General James Uthmeier, a Republican, filing a lawsuit in June.

The nonprofit Common Sense Media recently gave Google's AI overviews an "unacceptable risk" rating. Last month, Sens. Richard Blumenthal, D-Conn., and Josh Hawley, R-Mo., launched an inquiry over Google's push of AI into classrooms.

Google introduced AI overviews as a search function in May 2024.

— CNBC's MacKenzie Sigalos contributed to this report.
